# DECISIONS — where cafe keeps its existing choice, or deviates from the spec

One line of why each. HR = hard requirement in the spec.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Build and measure on homebase CPU (8 vCPU, no GPU); the compute backend is a config switch (`cpu` / `gpu`). | No GPU exists and Azure GPU quota is 0; HR "1 h on 8×H100" can't be verified until the quota request lands. |
| 2 | L1 stays WRF + HYCOM + WW3 as read today (`env.py`, `ocean.py`). | It is the existing, already-run environment model; one-way and read-only as the HR wants. |
| 3 | The existing Monte Carlo (`mission.run`) is stage 1, the ensemble — not rewritten. | Already Fossen 6-DOF, relative velocity, batched, with Wilson intervals and receipts. |
| 4 | Keep semi-implicit Euler instead of RK4. | The spec allows semi-implicit, and it is what the HR asks for with added mass; receipts pass on it. |
| 5 | Moving nests follow GO-MELT (Leonor & Wagner): fixed-size arrays per level that move with their source by whole cells, no remeshing, JIT kernels. | Matches the spec's HR "whole-cell shifts only", and fixed shapes are what let JAX compile once and run the same code on CPU or GPU. |
| 6 | Nest-solver choice (OpenFOAM vs a JAX lattice-Boltzmann LES) stays open until both are benchmarked; the planner takes per-solver throughput. | OpenFOAM is the solver in use (HR: benchmark the actual solver) but has no production GPU path; LBM is the candidate that does. |
| 7 | Quad dynamics step is 2.5 ms (was 10 ms), the other vehicles stay 20 ms. | HR multirate. Measured 2026-10-28 on the example quad: 10, 5, 2.5 and 1.25 ms give the same energy (±0.5 %) and hold error (±0.02 m), so 10 ms was already converged for it; 2.5 ms costs 3.2x the quad's wall time and buys headroom for stiffer frames. Receipt "Time step" re-checks 2.5 vs 1.25 ms. |
| 8 | Seawater density is TEOS-10 in situ from HYCOM T/S, applied to buoyancy only; drag, fin lift and wave pressure keep 1025 kg/m³. Gliders are re-ballasted neutral for their launch-point water (user's choice, 2026-10-28), keeping any offset the design had from neutral; other subs keep their mass. Hulls are rigid (no compressibility). T/S is one HYCOM snapshot nearest the start, held for the mission. | Buoyancy is where ±0.5 % of density matters: at TEPEX the 100 m water lifts a trimmed glider by 0.23 kg, 57 % of its 400 cc engine. In drag/lift it is a 0.5 % change, inside the CFD band. A real glider hull compresses and cancels part of the pressure term (~0.45 kg/m³ per 100 m), so rigid is slightly pessimistic for diving. One snapshot: tds.hycom.org took 330 s for four 3-hourly T/S times of a 1° box (over the 300 s deadline) and 39 s for one, and 3-hourly fields don't resolve the internal waves that move density faster anyway. |
| 9 | The surface drone stays a motor boat until a saildrone model exists. | The spec's surface drone is a saildrone; nothing in the repo has a sail. Logged as missing in STATUS. |
| 10 | Gliders keep the built-in controller (a stub in the spec's terms). | ArduSub has no buoyancy-engine or fin control; flagged in STATUS. |
| 11 | Planner self-check asserts the spec's air/ocean reference rows (one L2a-sized nest per medium, 20 % of budget, at 10⁹ cell-updates/s/GPU); hull rows are shown, not asserted. Real plans use `bench/throughput.json`. | Those four numbers reproduce to the spec's quoted digits. The spec's hull rows came from an L3 cost model it didn't state; the graded L3 here gives 1.5–2x different cells, and the spec text isn't on disk to reconcile. The 10⁹ default can be 5x off either way. |

## Pre-mission simulator (TEPEX-C build)

Branch `premission-step4`, written 2026-10-29. One row per choice the brief (`docs/BRIEF.md`) did not dictate: an alternative chosen, an assumed number, a convention, a place the brief was corrected by the data,
or a speed trade-off with its measurement. Numbering continues from the table above across the sub-tables. "Assumed" numbers are not repeated here; they are in `config/assumptions.yaml`,
`config/perturbations.yaml` (kind `assumed`) and the platform yamls (`assumed: true`), each with what would replace it. "Measured" means on homebase, 2026-10-29, unless a date is given.
The model equations behind each choice are in `docs/PHYSICS.md`.

### Data and ingest

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 12 | The demo window is 2023-03-01 12Z to 03-07 12Z (6 days, 145 hourly files), not the brief's 03-05 to 03-12 (`missions/tepex_c_demo.yaml`). | orca holds 03-01 00Z to 03-07 18Z only (163 hourly WRF and UMWM files; a HEAD probe of every hour, 03-07 19Z onward is 404); the first 12 h are WRF spin-up. The directory is `temp/`, so the run may grow; widen `window` when it does. |
| 13 | Read only the mission box plus `pad_deg` (2 deg) by HTTP byte range; read 3-D WRF (U, V, PH, PHB, T on the 8 lowest mass levels, about 21 to 400 m) only for the launch hour and the next of each UAS sortie; every other hour takes the wind from U10 through COARE. | WRF fields are deflate-compressed in quarter-domain chunks `(1, nz, 350, 825)` and the box straddles the y = 350 seam: about 4 MB per 2-D field and 80 MB per 3-D field per hour. Measured: 145 hours in 463 s, 14.2 GB transferred, 2.1 GB on disk. |
| 14 | Every file is checked against `cafe/schemas/{wrfout,umwmout,umwmrst}.json` (dimensions, dtype, units, shape, WRF global attributes, and for WRF `Times`) and refused on any difference. The `Times` check is WRF's only: a UMWM file carries no time in its content, so its hour is its file name. (amended by 556) | The brief's coupling-field list was wrong (the file has `UE_ESMF VE_ESMF UST_ESMF VST_ESMF WSPDREL PSIM_ESMF TAUX_ESMF TAUY_ESMF EPS_UMWM HS_UMWM`, plus `UST PBLH RAINC RAINSH TSK SST_INPUT SSTSK`); the schemas are written from a real file, so a changed layout cannot be misread silently. |
| 15 | The grid-to-earth rotation (COSALPHA, SINALPHA) is applied to U10 and V10 and unit-tested with a quarter turn, though it is the identity here; 3-D U and V are used without it. `wrf-python` is not used. | The grid is Mercator, COSALPHA = 1 and SINALPHA = 0 to float precision in the box. No `wrf-python` wheel exists for Python 3.12; the rotation is two lines (`conventions.rotate_grid_to_earth`) and heights are `(PH + PHB)/g` destaggered, as wrf-python does. Using 3-D winds unrotated is correct only on this grid. |
| 16 | Ingest the 6-hourly UMWM restart spectra `F(x, th, f)` for every 3rd sea point (36 km), and use the parametric spectrum between them. | The brief said the spectrum was absent; the restart files exist (2.5 GB each, CDF-1, `x` outermost so each box row is one byte range). Thinning bounds the cache; the real spectra are the check on the parametric fit (decision 34). |
| 17 | UMWM integrated fields are NaN on land (`seamask = 0`) and stay NaN (no data, not zero); `rhow` (constant 1029 kg/m3) is not used. | `cafe/schemas/umwmout.json`. Seawater density comes from TEOS-10 (decision 25). |
| 18 | Ship `config/certs/incommon_rsa_server_ca2.pem` and add it to the trust store for orca requests; certificate verification stays on. | orca serves the wrong intermediate (InCommon RSA Server CA, should be CA 2), so Linux curl and OpenSSL fail. The certificate (sha256 6e5a1f3c...0e2a) comes from the server certificate's own AIA URL and chains to the USERTrust root. |
| 19 | Remote readers refuse rather than guess: a ranged read that is not HTTP 206 is an error; a cut body is retried 3 times; a stalled zero-byte download raises and keeps its `.part`; a window with no restart hour or reversed dates is a `ValueError`; a file of 0 bytes is a file of 0 bytes, the 416 of the one-byte probe is its size, not a missing file. (amended by 665 and 710) | Each was a real failure found by a test (see `CHANGELOG.md`): a truncated header hung, a server ignoring `Range` returned whole files, a 0-byte stall looped. |
| 20 | Each hour is extracted to a per-hour `.npz` first, then assembled into Zarr; remote inputs are hashed by the bytes extracted. The disk cost is controlled by keeping the box subset only. | An interrupted ingest resumes where it stopped. homebase had 62 GB RAM and 19 GB free disk (93 % full) on 2026-10-29 (the brief assumed 32 GB and asked for 300 GB free), so only the box subset fits. The orca per-hour `.npz` cache (1.3 GB) was deleted after assembly (re-ingest takes about 8 min); the GOFS raw parts (2.4 GB) were kept because refetching takes about 2 h. |
| 21 | `python -m cafe.pm fetch` (and the ingest stage of `run`) downloads all four inputs, each only when not cached and still good: the orca WRF/UMWM hours (`orca.ingest`), the TAO/TRITON moorings (`tao.fetch_tao`), the GEBCO cut (`bathy.fetch_gebco`) and the GOFS 3.1 ocean store (`hycom.fetch_gofs`, about 2 h for the demo box). With `CAFE_OFFLINE` set nothing is downloaded and the missing input fails its check (the failed checks are `orca_present`, `tao_present`, `gebco_present` and `ocean_store_present`; each names the command to run with network access). A lab HYCOM store (`source: lab_hycom`) is not downloaded; `ingest-ocean` writes it. (amended by 421 and 465) | Rewritten in review round 1 (M-32). The first text said the fetchers were built as modules and left out of the pipeline, so `make fetch` left out the ocean and TAO; `pipeline.s_ingest` has called all four since `7a359dd`, and the CHANGELOG says so. |

### Environment

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 22 | Ocean source is GOFS 3.1 GLBy0.08 expt_93.0 (default) or the lab's HYCOM through the same Zarr layout; GOFS is fetched one 3-hourly step per request, each in a child process killed at a deadline (1500 s), servers tried in the caller's order (NCSS then OPeNDAP by default), with a SHA-256 manifest of the parts. (amended by 343, 352, 364 and 396) | Measured 2026-10-29 for the 14 x 34 deg, 40-level box: NCSS answered one step of temperature and salinity in 193 s at best and not within 600 s at worst; a 2-step request was cut with HTTP 500 after 302 s; OPeNDAP took 310 to 335 s per step and failed a 4-step read ("DAP failure"). One step per request loses only its own part when a request hangs. |
| 23 | `temperature_kind` (`in_situ` or `potential`) is mandatory on every ocean store and never guessed; GOFS GLBy is in situ. | hycom.org: GLBy writes in-situ temperature, only the old GLBa wrote potential. Reading in situ as potential makes the water 0.05 to 0.15 K too warm at 1000 m and the density 0.005 to 0.05 kg/m3 too small (receipts). The lab's HYCOM may differ. |
| 24 | The store has longitude in [0, 360), depth positive down, float64 coordinates and float32 fields loaded lazily one variable at a time; the protocol uses z positive up. (amended by 421) | The TEPEX-C box crosses 180 deg and [-180, 180) would split it. One variable over the box is about 1.2 GB. |
| 25 | Density and sound speed are TEOS-10 per cell (`p_from_z`, `SA_from_SP`, `CT_from_t` or `CT_from_pt`, `rho`, `sound_speed`), stored in the ocean store, and interpolated linearly in time between the 3-hourly steps. Row 8's one-snapshot rule stays for the existing app only. | Brief 1.3. The pre-mission path runs 6 days, not a 3 h mission; holding one snapshot would be wrong by days. |
| 26 | Lab HYCOM readers: netCDF per snapshot and `archv.*.a/.b` (`yrflag 3` only, curvilinear grids refused); total velocity = baroclinic + barotropic; layer thickness `thknss/9806`; layers to fixed depths by linear interpolation between layer mid-points. | Proven only on a synthetic fixture encoded from GOFS values (round trip within 5e-5); no lab file exists yet. A format the reader does not know is refused with the reason. |
| 27 | Bathymetry order is GEBCO_2026 15 arc-second, then the deepest valid GOFS level, then UMWM `depth`; each answer names its source and resolution. GEBCO is read by one byte range per box row (about 16 kB for the 34 deg TEPEX-C box, 8,160 int16 columns; 59 MB of the 7.47 GB file as first measured: 3,360 rows of 16,320 bytes are 54.8 MB, so the 59 MB is 7 % higher and was not re-derived). (amended by 495 and 723) | Brief 1.3 asked for a documented fallback order. The CEDA file is uncompressed and contiguous, so a row is a range. GEBCO's own download page has no scriptable interface. In a pipeline run only GEBCO and GOFS are tried (the UMWM depth is passed only when no ocean store exists). |
| 28 | Wind below the first WRF mass level (about 21 m), and at every hour with no 3-D field, is the COARE 3.5 stability-corrected profile scaled to U10; above 21 m in sortie hours it is the WRF 3-D wind, linear in ln z, constant above the top stored level. (Amended in review round 4, see 251: nothing is used above the top stored level.) | 3-D fields cost 80 MB per field-hour, so they are read only where a vehicle flies. COARE u* agrees with WRF `UST` (0.208 against 0.211 and 0.159 against 0.168 m/s at two sample points). |
| 29 | COARE 3.5 runs in sea-state mode (`z0 = 0.091 Hs (u*/cp)^2`, floored at `0.11 nu/u*`) with UMWM `dcp` and `swh`, and where it differs from `pycoare` 0.4.3 the paper wins. (amended by 653) | pycoare passes a truthy object as `setup` in its in-loop `_get_charn`, so it never subtracts `0.11 nu/u*` in sea-state mode. The difference is up to 5 % in u* and 10 % in Cd on the test grid; the two are shown as context rows, not pass criteria. |
| 30 | The Cd receipt is a closure (a root-find of the same z0 law), listed as a known limitation. | Edson et al.'s Cd table could not be read (HTTP 403), so there was no independent number to compare with. |
| 31 | WRF SST is treated as the foundation temperature and the COARE warm layer (NOAA `coare36vnWarm_et`, re-implemented) is added on top; its boundary-layer height is the per-cell mean PBLH. The source text of the WRF data and of the SST states what the file's attributes say (`SST_UPDATE`, `SIMULATION_START_DATE`, `DX`), not a literal; the demo's text is unchanged. (amended by 722) | `SST_UPDATE = 0`, so the WRF SST is frozen at 1 March with no diurnal cycle. The layer reaches 3.13 K in the calm of 03-05/06, the top of the 0.5 to 3 K range. z_i enters weakly. |
| 32 | The Stokes drift integral is the deep-water Kenyon form in the angular-frequency convention, `u_s(0) = (16 pi^3/g) int f^3 E df`; the brief's "2 m3/g" is read with `m3 = int omega^3 E(omega) d omega`. | The brief's expression only holds in that convention (Breivik et al. 2016 eq. 6); receipt 0.1688 against 0.1687 m/s. |
| 33 | The spectrum from UMWM parameters is two components: the primary (DHH 1985 wind sea, or a JONSWAP-shaped swell when cp/U10 exceeds 1.2) and a short DHH wind sea at U10/cp = 2.0, whose variance share is the closed form that makes the spectrum's mean-square slope equal the file's `mss`; the spreading is Mitsuyasu/Hasselmann, capped at s = 60. | The brief asked to fit s to `mss`, which is impossible: UMWM's `mss = sum F k^3 dk dtheta` integrates over direction (BRIEF correction §2.3). The synthesised matches the file's to 0.0 % where the short-wave share is not clipped; over the 906 sampled sea points of the demo cache the 90th-percentile error is 35 % and the share is clipped at 31 % of them (ledger check `spectrum_mss_vs_file`, limit 50 %). The swell threshold 1.2 and the short-sea age 2.0 are assumed and are code defaults in `waves.py`; they are not yet in `assumptions.yaml`. |
| 34 | The hourly Stokes drift is the parametric-spectrum field multiplied by the complex ratio real/fit at the 6-hourly restart times, interpolated linearly in time; its uncertainty tag is the leave-one-out median speed error. (amended by 641 and 658) | On the real cache the fit alone had a median speed error of 38 % the median over all nodes of abs(fit/real - 1) (the attribute `fit_only_median_speed_err`, 0.3843); the first draft also said the fit under-predicted by about x2.7, which no ledger holds: withdrawn in round 12 (row 658), where the receipt's median fit/real ratio of 0.657 on the real cache is recorded. The correction gives a leave-one-out median of 5.1 % in speed and 3.7 deg in direction. Using a median error as a 1-sigma is an assumption. |
| 35 | The second-order sea surface is the deep-water limit of Sharma & Dean 1979 derived in `_sd_coeffs`, applied to the 64 largest components above `kp Hs/2 = 0.05`. | No source with the deep-water coefficients was in hand; the derivation is checked against the Stokes crest and the Longuet-Higgins & Stewart collinear limits. It needs review. The 0.05 threshold is the brief's (Smit et al. 2017 is not verified). Not called by Stage 1 or 3. |
| 36 | Sub-grid turbulence is the Mann (1994) box via `mannrs` 2.0.0 (IEC Annex C: Gamma 3.9, L = 0.8 Lambda_1, Lambda_1 = 0.7 z capped at 42 m), rescaled to sigma_u = 2.5 u*; the stability scaling is `1/phi_m(z/L)` clipped to [0.2, 3]. | The box resolves kL up to about 3 only, so sigma_v/sigma_u is 0.63 and sigma_w/sigma_u 0.43 against the IEC 0.8 and 0.5; the clip and the rule (Pena, Gryning & Mann 2010) are assumed and unvalidated offshore. Stage-3 LES calibration (`config/subgrid.json` `calibration`) is the intended correction. |
| 37 | Gust fronts are a Poisson process in the hour with rate `lambda_bg + lambda_max (1 - exp(-rain/R0)) f(theta_e)`; peak dU uniform 3 to 10 m/s, duration uniform 10 to 30 min, dT fixed at -1.5 K. The pipeline omits `theta_e` (f = 1) until `7a359dd`; since then it passes `theta_e` from the ship's T2, Q2 and PSFC (`stage1._uas_forcing`), so f is active (amended by 332). | Wills, Cronin & Zhang 2021 (abstract only) give 382 events and the 1.5 K, 10 min detection rule but no rate; the rate model and its constants are assumed. Omitting f gave the rain-only upper rate. |
| 38 | The rain lens is 0.5 psu fresher over the top 0.5 m, strongest below 5 m/s and absent above 8 m/s, and acts on density only; it persists after the rain with an e-folding time of 10 min to 2 h set by wind. | Iyer & Drushka 2021, summarised in the brief and not re-read. Trigger, saturation, e-folding times and the -0.2 K are assumed. Persistence (`last_rain_mm_h`, derived-store layout 2) was added in commit bb68642; before it the lens vanished the hour the rain ended. |
| 39 | The diurnal jet is a surface-trapped 0.2 m/s (the midpoint of the 0.1 to 0.3 range) along the wind, scaled by `min(dT_warm/3 K, 1)` and falling linearly to zero at the warm-layer depth. Windage is not added to the current. (amended by 650) | TEPEX-C's own hypothesis (brief 2.2), modelled, not observed. The HYCOM top layer already contains the Ekman drift, the USV VPP has its own windage and the glider adds its own leeway at the surface (1.8-2.3 % per member, row 101), so adding windage again would count it twice. Every member gets the midpoint: the range is not sampled per member and the jet carries no uncertainty in the run; `subgrid.diurnal_jet` draws it per member and only the receipts call it. Sampling it would shift USV and glider P(fail) and is left to the owner (round 12, M-10, row 650). |
| 40 | The warm layer and rain lens change density by `rho (-alpha dT - beta dS)` with TEOS-10 alpha and beta at the fixed SA 35, CT 28, p 0 (tropical constants, not the box's mean state: outside the tropics `density_extra` is too large by the alpha ratio, 2.8 times at 5 degC; the demo, SST 299-303 K, is unaffected). (amended by 656) | A linearised correction needs two coefficients. The choice of SA 35, CT 28 is the builder's; the code gives no further reason and does not adapt it to the box (round 12, M-16, row 656; this row used to call it the box's mean surface state). |
| 41 | Superseded by rows 100 and 103. The mission's `warm_layer`, `rain_lens` and `ocean_sde` switches are now read (`subgrid.switch`), the warm layer, rain lens and diurnal jet enter through `SurfaceLayer`, and the ocean SDE moves the vehicles (row 100). | At the time of this row the functions existed and were receipted but no stage called them. |
| 42 | `PerturbedEnv` applies the Stage-1 errors per member: wind speed plus a bias (floored at 0) and rotated clockwise; current speed times a lognormal and rotated, with half of each prior's variance member-wide (the Latin-hypercube draw) and half a random Fourier field (32 features, Gaussian covariance, 100 km); Hs times a scale and Tp plus a bias; Stokes drift times Hs-scale squared. | The brief gave sigmas, not a spatial structure. Splitting the variance keeps the sensitivity indices informative and still gives a spatially correlated error. Rahimi & Recht 2007 random features need no grid. |
| 43 | The Tagged 1-sigma for T2, q2, PSFC, SWDOWN, GLW, wave direction and SST is assumed (`env.sigma.*`); wind, Hs and Tp take the Stage-1 priors. | No comparison against buoys exists for those fields. Only wind speed and direction are re-fitted from TAO (decision 45); the `assumptions.yaml` text for T2 says TAO overwrites it, and the code does not. |
| 44 | TAO/TRITON data come from NDBC's THREDDS copy of OceanSITES (10-minute WIND, SST, AIRT, RH); QC flags kept are {1, 2, 5} in delayed mode and {1, 2} in real time; winds are lifted from 4 m to 10 m with COARE where AIRT, RH and SST exist, else the neutral log law. (amended by 491 and 492) | The PMEL ERDDAP serves only daily means for 2023 and `tao_flux_absolute` ends in 2021-09. Delayed-mode files flag every calibrated sample 5 (50863 of 51027 on T0N165E). The neutral lift overstates the 4 to 10 m shear by 2 to 3 % at 6 m/s. The files have no wave variable, so UMWM `swh` is not validated against buoys. |
| 45 | The fitted wind priors are `N(-0.62, 1.33) m/s` for speed and `N(+7.2, 23.7) deg` for direction: the whole residual sd, hourly scatter included, becomes a member-wide persistent bias (amended in round 2, see 182: the sd is split into a persistent and a short-lived part). (amended by 559) | Real data, 2023-03-01 to 07, 11 moorings, 1492 hourly pairs: WRF minus TAO speed bias +0.62 m/s (rmse 1.46), direction bias -7.2 deg (rmse 24.8). `PerturbedEnv` has no time-varying wind error, so this is conservative; the between-mooring sd of the mean bias alone is about 0.36 m/s. SST bias -0.11 K (rmse 0.26) is recorded, not fed back. |

### Vehicles

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 46 | Seaglider drag is the basestation3 form `D = l^2 q (b q^s + c alpha^2)` with alpha in degrees, and the steady balance is `D = B sin(theta)`, `L = -B cos(theta)`, not the brief's. The battery is 17 MJ, not 10. | BRIEF correction §2.4: the brief's form leaves a 3.7e-3 force residual and is dimensionally inconsistent with `hd_b ~ q^(1/4)`; the basestation3 form closes to 1e-9. Kongsberg's pack is 17 MJ. |
| 47 | Slocum flight is the Merckelbach form as in `gliderflight` 1.2.1, checked against it as an oracle; the Seaglider has no oracle run here (basestation3's `HydroModel.py` could not be executed). | Slocum agrees with the oracle to 8e-5 (tolerance 2e-3, the oracle's own table). The Seaglider horizontal speed comes out at 0.228 m/s against 0.25 (Eriksen 2001; tolerance 15 %). |
| 48 | Gliders carry a pilot trim: dive and climb VBD settings `vbd_delta_cc` (60 cc, assumed) beyond the lightest and heaviest ballast points on an 11-depth profile, and the vehicle mass shifts to centre the span when it does not fit the 850 cc pump range. | A real pilot sets separate dive and climb VBD; a single neutral setting would give no flight. Row 8 ballasted for launch water only. |
| 49 | The glider temperature profile is an exponential `t_deep + (SST - t_deep) exp(-z/150 m)` with t_deep 4 degC, all assumed; it only enters through the thermal expansion of the hull. (amended by 659) | The Environment API does not expose the ocean store's temperature: `Ocean.temperature` exists but is not wired to the protocol, `Environment` or the perturbed wrapper. Wiring it (and sourcing T_ref and alpha_T) replaces the profile (round 12, M-19, row 659). Limitation L06. |
| 50 | Glider heading is fixed per dive and steered by the depth-averaged current of the previous dive; surfaced drift is the surface current plus 1 % of the 10 m wind; `station_lost` fires after `lost_after_h` outside the tolerance (the demo sets 12 h; the platform default is 6 h); the demo's `glider_eq` dives to 950 m. | Eriksen 2001 dive-averaged-current steering. The 1 % windage is the order used for low-windage drifters. The rating is 1000 m and ordering 1000 m trips `overdepth` by the apogee overshoot. |
| 51 | The pump energy is `p dV/eta` with eta 0.40, hotel load 0.5 W and surface comms 3.5 W, all assumed. | Bracketed by NOC (65 W for 2 min, 7.8 kJ at 1000 m) and Copernicus os-2019-36 (1172 J for a +-21 cc apogee pump; 0.136 W for a low-power dive). |
| 52 | The USV is a 4-DOF steady-state VPP (Fossen form, Xiao & Jouffroy 2014 structure), not Jouffroy's Simulink model. Current enters through the apparent wind; the Stage-1 simulator reads a still-water polar at the wind relative to the water. | Jouffroy's model was not available, so the structure is checked by force-balance residuals and limiting cases. Sun et al. 2026 say current is not superposable; solving it coupled is the model's own answer, and the table is the same solution (max difference 0.0017 m/s). |
| 53 | Wave added resistance is closed-form strip theory (Gerritsma & Beukelman in the Journee & Massie form), not Capytaine, with oblique seas scaled by `max(-cos chi, 0)`. | A 7 m slender hull follows waves longer than about 30 m (heave RAO tends to 1), the whole table builds in milliseconds, and a BEM call per (omega, heading, speed) would dominate the build for no visible change. The oblique factor has no source. |
| 54 | The USV parasitic drag area is calibrated (`appendage_cda_m2` raised from 0.02 to 0.04) so the best-heading speed at 12 m/s is 2.92 against Chiodi's 2.9 m/s; the receipt is labelled a calibration. | Without it the model was too fast at high wind. A fit is not a validation (limitation L02). |
| 55 | The USV VPP bias is the Stage-1 draw from `perturbations.yaml` (uniform -0.35 to -0.30); the platform's N(-0.30, 0.03) is used only when a caller passes none. | The brief gives 30 to 35 % overprediction as a range; a uniform on it keeps both ends. |
| 56 | `becalmed` means the best through-water speed over all headings is below the current speed for `becalmed_after_h`; the capsize proxy fires at a peak roll of 45 deg, heel is depowered to 25 deg, and the wing is stalled below 1.5 m/s apparent wind or 15 deg apparent angle for 30 min. | A literal "SOG below current" cannot fire, because a drifting boat's SOG equals the current. The three angles are assumed operating limits. |
| 57 | The UAS airframe is NeuroBEM (0.772 kg) with the Faessler et al. 2018 linear rotor drag used to 12 m/s and the air-relative velocity `v - wind`; the paper has no wind, so that step is ours. The battery is 60 Wh. | NeuroBEM and Faessler used different airframes; the drag is the best published for a quad of this class. 60 Wh sizes a 20 min sortie to land near 30 % charge and is not physically carried by 0.772 kg (the yaml says so). |
| 58 | UAS propulsion is parametric: `T = kT omega^2`, power `kQ omega^3` from a figure of merit of 0.60, maximum static thrust 33 N, omega_max 30 000 rpm, ESC efficiency 0.80, a 33 ms first-order lag. | No dyno curve exists for the lab's motor and propeller. Four of the six numbers are assumed. Limitation L11. |
| 59 | The UAS dynamics step is 2.5 ms, the environment, guidance and velocity loop 50 ms; the arithmetic is float32 in JAX; the Peukert exponent is 1.05. | Row 7 for the step. float32 is twice as fast as float64 here and leaves the process-wide x64 flag alone; the 480 k-step sums are accumulated per 50 ms block and are good to 1e-5. |
| 60 | Sorties launch at `local_solar_time` (UTC + lon/15 h) from a ship held on station; the mission's altitudes, duration and launch rules override the platform; a launch is scrubbed if the mean wind at flight height exceeds 12 m/s or a gust front is active within 15 min; a member whose every launch is scrubbed gets the flag `no_sortie_flown`. | Brief section 6. A scrubbed sortie yields no flux profile, and that is a failure of the mission, not a pass. |
| 61 | ArduCopter SITL is told it is at Canberra (149.165 E, 35.363 S); only metres from home are used. `WP_YAW_BEHAVIOR` is 0. | The firmware's compass and EKF misbehave at the equator. With the nose turning toward each leg the EKF flagged a GPS/compass glitch and the return diverged in 3 of 4 trials; with a fixed heading it did not. The return and landing are still unreliable (limitation L10). |
| 62 | The Mann turbulence for a sortie is built from the fewest boxes that hold the members (non-overlapping x-segments), turbulence and gusts at 50 ms and the mean wind looked up every 1 s. | Members separated by at least U x duration along a box are uncorrelated (receipt: rms zero-lag correlation below 0.1); the fewest boxes cost the least (a box takes seconds). Profiling showed the gridded wind lookup was most of the wall time. |

### Comms

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 63 | The acoustic budget is Thorp absorption plus Stojanovic spreading (k = 1.5) and noise, with the implementation loss derived so that the budget closes at the vendor's published range with 3 dB to spare at 5 m/s wind; `SL = 190 dB` and `SNR_req = 10 dB` are assumed and cancel. (amended by 608) | The vendor gives only a range (`>= 4 km` MF). The 4 km row is true by construction (limitation L01); the 6 and 8 km rows test the shape. At 10 m/s the MF margin at 4 km is -3.9 dB. The Micro-Modem bands copy the Benthos MF loss. |
| 64 | Bellhop runs only where the Thorp margin is within +-6 dB of zero, on a 48-point range grid, and enters as the excess TL over an isovelocity column added to the Thorp TL. Bellhop was built from OALIB `at_2026_10_27` (sha 00b100a8...). (amended by 608) | An absolute Bellhop TL would double-count the spreading because the implementation loss is anchored under k = 1.5 and deep isovelocity water is k = 2 (so k = 1.5 is optimistic). When Bellhop is absent the link is labelled "Thorp only, Bellhop unavailable". |
| 65 | RF is a two-ray model with exact path lengths, reflection coefficient -1 (the error against the Fresnel coefficient is stated in row 652), an Ament roughness factor with sigma_h = Hs/4 on the specular ray only, and a 4/3-earth horizon beyond which the margin is -200 dB. The member's own Hs sets the roughness (for an RF link between two nodes, the larger of the two ends', row 651). (Amended in review round 4, see 244: the geometry is a 4/3-radius earth with a divergence factor, and the plane earth stands in near the horizon.) (amended by 651 and 652) | The brief's two-ray formula. Diffuse scatter and antenna motion are not modelled, so null filling is underestimated. Antenna gain 2 dBi and 3 dB miscellaneous loss are assumed. The RFD900x row uses its lowest-rate sensitivity (-121 dBm); `rfd900x_fast` uses -105 dBm (a retailer table). |
| 66 | Iridium is availability and latency only: 340 B SBD sessions, success 0.95 per attempt, 3 attempts, log-normal set-up (median 10 s, log sigma 0.6), 60 s per failed attempt, one independent draw per 10-minute moment. | No published SBD success statistic was found; all of these are assumed. Replace with the lab's SBDIX logs. |
| 67 | A link is judged only at the moments it is needed (`surfacing`, `uas_airborne`, `always`) at each member's own position, and succeeds when the working fraction reaches `required_fraction`; a link never needed counts as working. | Brief section 6. The demo's glider-to-ship acoustic links fail in every member because the vehicles are about 550 km from the ship (`QUESTIONS_FOR_LAB.md` 12). |

### Statistics

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 68 | Intervals are Wilson at every n (changed in review round 1, see 152; the first build switched to Agresti-Coull above 40); the verdict is GO if success >= 0.90 and its 90 % lower bound >= 0.75, NO-GO below 0.60, else MARGINAL. | These are the existing app's thresholds (`mission._verdict`). The app uses Wilson at every n, and so does this tool. |
| 69 | A run says when it cannot certify: 73 members are needed for a zero-failure two-sided Wilson upper bound below 5 % at 95 % (59 one-sided, 60 by the rule of three), and with zero failures below that the verdict carries "P(fail) < 3/n". Budget `quick` (at most 64 members) cannot certify. (amended by 286) | The brief asks for honesty about sample size. The number is computed, not typed (receipt). |
| 70 | Stage 1 stops when the whole 95 % interval on P(fail) is at or below 0.10 (GO) or above 0.40 (NO-GO) for every vehicle and link, not before `n_min` (8, 32, 73, 73 for ci, quick, 4h, overnight), and at `n_max` (16, 64, 256, 512) otherwise. Wald's SPRT is implemented and tested and not used. | The interval rule never contradicts the final verdict (tested). The SPRT has controlled error but needs a cut-off and an indifference region per verdict; the interval rule is simpler to explain and to audit. |
| 71 | Each batch is its own Latin hypercube, antithetic on `wind_speed_bias` and `current_speed_scale`; seeds for the current random fields and the refit drag are keyed by member and stream name, never by vehicle. The UAS turbulence, gust fronts and Iridium sessions are keyed by the batch's first member. | The two dominant forcing errors are mirrored to cut variance; common random numbers make every vehicle in member m fly the same perturbed environment, so link checks are consistent. The batch keying of the last three is a known issue (`CHANGELOG.md`). |
| 72 | Sensitivity is the EASI first-order index (Plischke 2010) with the 2012 bias correction, on the 0/1 failure indicator, only where both outcomes occur and n >= 16. | It needs no extra model runs, works on a Latin hypercube, and handles an indicator. It ranks parameters; it carries no confidence interval. |
| 73 | Subset simulation (Au & Beck 2001) is implemented and tested against a known Gaussian tail and is not called by the pipeline; its reported c.o.v. underestimated the spread (fixed in review round 7, M-26: the chain-correlation factor was taken on the wrong indicator); it refuses an n and p0 that leave no seed or a chain shorter than 2. (amended by 366 and 726) | Present for rare-event queries once a model is cheap enough. Stage 1 estimates P(fail) directly. |
| 74 | The emulator is SMT Kriging (KPLS above 10 features) on the logit scale with normalised split-conformal intervals and a range guard (99th percentile of leave-one-out nearest-neighbour distance); co-kriging is used only with 3 or more high-fidelity rows; the table falls back to CSV when `pyarrow` is missing. The pipeline stores hold-out scores only. (amended by 401) | SMT provides both kriging and Kennedy-O'Hagan co-kriging in one library. The conformal level holds for exchangeable rows whatever the GP does. Its receipts are scored on a synthetic table. |
| 75 | Priors that the brief lists without a magnitude are in `config/perturbations.yaml` with `kind: assumed` (battery capacity, lognormal sigma 0.05); `bulk_stress_scale` was listed and no module applied it (row 102 now applies it to the UAS Mann-turbulence amplitude). | A prior of kind `assumed` caps two NASA factors in the credibility report (`docs/CREDIBILITY.md`). |

### Stage 3

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 76 | The window engine is a native JAX D3Q19 kernel (regularised BGK, Smagorinsky, Bouzidi curved walls, Ladd moving walls, Davies sponge); XLB 0.3.1 is kept for the reference cases. (amended by 603) | XLB 0.3.1 has no wall-model boundary, no moving nest and no vmappable boundary state. XLB's Smagorinsky uses `36 Cs^2` where Hou et al. 1996 derive `18 sqrt2 Cs^2`, so its Cs = 0.17 acts as 0.202; `XLB_CS_SCALE` converts and a test compares them. |
| 77 | Smagorinsky Cs = 0.17 in Hou's form, lattice speed U_LAT = 0.04 (0.05 for the sphere runs), viscosity floored at nu_lat = 1e-4, fp16 storage only on a GPU, JAX memory factor 2.5 per cell. | Ma = 0.069 leaves 44 % local overshoot before 0.1. Below nu_lat 1e-4 the float32 BGK went NaN on the CI quad window; a window that needed the floor is reported failed with the reason. fp16 pays only where bandwidth is the limit. The JAX path measured 182 B per cell (1.96 times FluidX3D's layout); the nest adds masks, so 2.5. |
| 78 | A window runs in the vehicle frame (grid fixed on the body, inflow = environment minus vehicle velocity). The ground-frame nest that shifts by whole cells (row 5) is kept, receipted, and not used for coefficients. | Galilean-exact for constant velocity and needs no shift. Each whole-cell shift displaces fluid without pushing it, which measured as a spurious force of about -2 times the drag at one shift per 25 steps. |
| 79 | The wall model is the equilibrium log law (kappa 0.41, B 5.2) at a matching point 2.5 cells off the wall, with specular reflection on flat faces, the log-law stress removed from the wall cell, and `nu_t = kappa u_tau y` below the matching point. | Measured alternatives that failed: a no-slip wall with a tuned viscosity (1.3 to 2.6 times the log-law stress), a tuned slip velocity (noise), a free-slip wall without near-wall viscosity (C_f 70 % low). Smagorinsky's `Cs dx` is 6 times too short at one cell. |
| 80 | The wall model is receipted in a body-force channel (wall stress = g h exactly; bulk C_f against Dean 1978 within 10 %) and, at Re_L >= 1e6, on a flat plate against ITTC-57 within 10 % from a 1.8 h stored run. A window's `wall_model_receipt_error` takes the larger (worse) stored error (row 104; the first build took the smaller). | The first flat-plate receipt compared a laminar CI-size plate with a turbulent correlation and was 71 % low: ill-posed. The channel has an exact reference and runs in about 15 s. Taking the smaller of two stored errors would have been lenient, which is why row 104 takes the worse. Limitation L09. |
| 81 | A window's resolution is the 10-cells-per-boundary-layer value coarsened by the fourth root of the budget shortfall, never below 2 cells across the body or 3 box transits; it is cut to its wall-clock slice; `ok` needs every self-check, and only `ok` rows enter the refit. | Cells scale as dx^-3 and steps as dx^-1, so the fourth root fits both. No unvalidated coefficient may reach Stage 1b. The box (3 x 1.6 x 1.6 body lengths), the cells, the spin-up share (0.3) and every dimension the yamls lack are assumed (`stage3.*`). |
| 82 | The refit pools the per-window ratio `cd/cd_hangar` with DerSimonian-Laird random effects and gives Stage 1 a lognormal multiplier with the pooled mean and `sqrt(se^2 + tau^2)`; speed scales as `d^(-1/2)` for the glider and USV and the UAS takes d directly; the ci budget never refits. | Windows are different conditions, so their means differ by more than their errors. Speed follows drag^-1/2 at fixed propulsive force (drag ~ v^2). A first version divided every window by the first window's coefficient and used the mean as the median (fixed, `CHANGELOG.md`). |
| 83 | FluidX3D is disabled by default and is on only for the JSON boolean `true` (a quoted 'false' or a 1 is refused with a message); GPU windows use XLB-Warp, and the CPU fallback is labelled in the report. (amended by 735) | FluidX3D's licence forbids commercial and military use and requires publishing modifications (`docs/LICENSES.md`); NOAA use is `QUESTIONS_FOR_LAB.md` 8. |

### Pipeline and planner

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 84 | The pipeline is ingest, hangar, stage 1, windows, stage 3, refit, stage 1b, emulator, report; each stage writes a ledger with the sha256 of its inputs and outputs and names the previous ledger as an input; a stage is reused on resume only if its ledger is clean, its config hash matches, its outputs are unchanged, and no earlier stage reran. The hash includes a sha256 of `cafe/pm/**/*.py`, `config/` and `platforms/`. (amended by 290 and 479) | `make audit` walks the chain. Any code change reruns every stage: the safe reading of "same inputs", at the price of redoing a 6-day Stage 1 after a one-line edit. `res.pkl` is a private checkpoint format whose hash the ledger re-checks. |
| 85 | The budget plan is wall-clock: ci 10 min, quick 20 min, 4h, overnight 10 h; 45 % of it to Stage 1, 30 % to Stage 3, 15 % to Stage 1b; batches of 16, 32, 64, 128; windows per vehicle 1, 1, 3, 3; SITL members 0, 0, 4, 8. (amended by 310) | The brief's contract is the budget, not a machine. Cost is per environment call, not per member, so batches are large. A plan that does not fit is shrunk (members, then windows, then resolution) and says so. |
| 86 | Stage-1 cost comes from `config/throughput.json` (written by `bench/throughput.py`): measured on homebase at load 33, glider 7.7 s per batch per day + 0.157 s per member per day, USV 12.6 + 0, UAS 55.7 + 0; labelled fallbacks (8, 0.1), (11, 0.1), (20, 0.3) are used and flagged when a class is not measured. LBM rate: measured 7.5e6 cell-updates/s on CPU, 5e6 fallback; a GPU rate is a projection (0.95 x 20.8e9 per device) and says so. | The benchmark was taken under load; real rates on an idle box will be better. No GPU exists (Azure quota 0), so any GPU figure is a labelled projection. |
| 87 | `grid.interp` gathers all 2^d corners in one flat index take. | Stage 1 calls it about 10^6 times on a handful of points, where per-call overhead is all the cost: 2.3 to 3.5 times faster per call, identical to 4e-16. |
| 88 | The UAS runs one jitted `lax.scan` per 1 s record interval (20 blocks of 50 ms) instead of one call per block, and the stage-1 batches were enlarged. | A Python round trip and device sync per 50 ms block was most of a sortie's wall time. CI Stage 1 (16 members, the real 6-day mission) went 969 s, then 386 s, then 295 s. Glider and USV: 256 members for 7 days in 4.6 s and 18 s (replan 300 s). |
| 89 | Stage-3 windows are chosen from Stage-1 tracks: the K record times of strongest forcing per vehicle (10 m wind for UAS and the USV wing, Hs for the USV hull, current speed for gliders), at least 6 h apart, from the failed members if any, else all. | The windows should be where the model is stressed. The first version crashed on a vehicle with no finite position (fixed). |
| 90 | The report's JSON is the source of truth; every number is a node `{value, unit, stage, source, uncertainty, receipt, ledger}`, the HTML is rendered from it, and the report stage fails if a number does not trace or does not appear in the HTML. | Brief exit condition 2. The credibility page is built in the same stage (`docs/CREDIBILITY.md`). |

### Receipts and CI

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 91 | `python -m cafe.pm receipts` normalises every record to one row `{id, module, name, value, reference, tolerance, unit, ok, kind, source, note}`; a bracket's reference is its middle and its tolerance its half width; a receipt function that raises or records nothing is a FAIL row; a context row never counts. | A required check that did not happen must not read as fine. |
| 92 | Slow receipts (the emulator at n = 500, SITL and the 20 min sortie, the app's CalculiX FEA) run under `--slow`, are stored with the git sha and a code digest in `$CAFE_DATA/receipts`, and are read back by a default run; a required group with no result, or one made by different code, is a FAIL row `<group>:not_run` with the command and run time. A stored FAIL stays a FAIL. (amended by 348) | Tens of minutes each. The digest covers the whole `cafe/pm` tree, config and platforms, so any edit stales them: a finer file list would miss a dependency. |
| 93 | The wave-direction receipts run on a synthetic trade-wind fixture built with plain cos and sin (no call into `conventions`) and, when a mission cache exists, on the real cache. `--broken tests/fixtures/broken/wave_direction.yaml` gives UMWM data the WW3 convention and the exit code is inverted: 0 only if a receipt failed. | The expected directions must not depend on the code under test. CI runs the broken configuration to show the receipts can fail (brief exit condition 3). Several bounds are ours (25 deg for wave-wind alignment, 30 deg for the parametric against the real Stokes direction): twice what the TEPEX-C cache gives. |
| 94 | Credibility levels run 0 to 4 and are computed from files that exist; level 4 is never awarded; a known limitation that bears on a factor caps it at 2; the rule behind each level is printed next to it. | The 0-4 scale is the brief's; the rule for each level is this tool's own reading, because NASA-STD-7009B was not re-read. See `docs/CREDIBILITY.md`. |
| 95 | (Grid pair superseded by 120.) The DTMB 5415 validation uses the SIMMAN 2008 IGES hull lofted by a custom NURBS evaluator (the patches overlap and OpenCASCADE cannot sew them), held at the static draft, fresh water at 20 C, with U_D, U_S and U_nu assumed; the grid pair has r = 1.30. Hydrostatics are receipts (displacement 1 %, Lwl 0.5 %, Bwl 1 %; the stored values are -0.14, -0.11 and +0.13 %). | The DTIC report (ADA458075) answered HTTP 403, so the four C_T values are the brief's and the experimental uncertainties are assumed. The free surface is fixed, so sinkage and trim sit in E. |
| 96 | Grid convergence uses Celik et al. 2008 as written (signed equation 3, `brentq` fallback, r = (N_fine/N_coarse)^(1/3), a flag when r < 1.3); two-grid GCI uses Roache's Fs = 3 and an assumed order. (amended by 369) | Reproduces Celik's Table 1 in all three columns. The existing app had assumed r = 2 (fixed). |
| 97 | `make audit` bans the markers listed in `docs/BUILDERS.md` (unfinished-work tags, the stand-in word, test-exclusion and expected-failure markers, commented-out code) in `cafe/pm`, `tests`, `bench`, `platforms`, `missions`, `config`, `docs/RUNBOOK.md` and the Makefile; gates coverage at 90 % for `cafe/pm` and 100 % for `physics`, `stats` and `env`; runs ruff and mypy. CI (`.github/workflows/premission.yml`) runs audit, `make ci` on the synthetic environment (`tests/fixtures/ci_env`, no network) and the broken configuration. (amended by 291 and 609) | `docs/BUILDERS.md`. A missing optional binary becomes a labelled fallback path that is itself tested. |
| 98 | Verification of the existing app's physics is by manufactured solutions and by vendored reference terms: the implicit-midpoint Coriolis is tested against its exact law, and the Fossen mass and Coriolis terms against Fossen's PythonVehicleSimulator (REMUS 100 and Otter) to 1e-9; the app keeps no roll added inertia where the reference adds 0.3 Ix, and keeps eight Munk terms the reference zeroes. | The differences are exposed by the tests, not hidden: the roll term is a gap; keeping the Munk terms is the builder's judgement of the correct physics. |
| 99 | `fetch` (and the ingest stage of `run`) downloads all four inputs: orca WRF/UMWM, GOFS 3.1, TAO/TRITON and the GEBCO cut. Each one is skipped when it is cached and its hash still matches. With `CAFE_OFFLINE` set (any value except empty or 0) nothing is downloaded, and every missing input fails `<name>_present`, naming `python -m cafe.pm fetch --mission <path>`. `tests/conftest.py` sets it for every test. | A run should never download without saying so, and an offline box should name the one command that completes it. A missing GEBCO cut fails the check even though `bathy.choose` could fall back to the GOFS floor, because a silent fallback would let the report cite the wrong bathymetry. |
| 100 | The ocean SDE is a per-step position displacement, dK/dx dt + sqrt(2K dt) xi (`PerturbedEnv.sde_displacement`, one Euler-Maruyama step of `subgrid.ocean_sde_step`), keyed by member and indexed by step number. The glider and USV add it to their position update only. The current they read, their steering, the glider's dive-averaged current, and every failure test (becalmed, wing_stall, apparent wind) never see it. K is Okubo's value at the 0.08 deg HYCOM cell and is the same at every depth. | The first version added it to `PerturbedEnv.current` as a velocity, a ~0.48 m/s kick every 60 s step that the USV sensed. That reset its becalmed timer and jittered its apparent wind: on the real mission, becalmed fell from 15 to 0 of 32 members and wing_stall from 10 to 1. As a position step the modes are back (becalmed 15, swept 28, wing_stall 9 of 32) and the dispersion is still 2Kt. Leaving it out of the glider's DAC is a simplification: a real GPS-based DAC would carry ~0.04 m/s of it over a 3 h dive, well below the current prior's sigma. |
| 101 | Windage: none added for the USV, because its VPP already carries the above-water CdA. The gliders' surface drift uses a per-member fraction drawn uniformly from 1.8-2.3 %. | Adding the brief's 1.8-2.3 % on top of the VPP would count USV windage twice. |
| 102 | `bulk_stress_scale` scales only the UAS Mann-turbulence amplitude (sigma_u = 2.5 u*, u*^2 = stress, so each member's gust series is multiplied by sqrt(scale)). It does not touch the length scale or the mean profile. | The Mann turbulence is the only consumer of surface stress in the pipeline. |
| 103 | The sub-grid switches warm_layer, rain_lens and ocean_sde default to on when the mission leaves them out. turbulence and gust_fronts default to off. A switch never changes `derived.zarr`; it is applied when the store is read. (amended by 282) | The brief makes the warm layer default on. Applying switches at read time means one cache serves every mission variant. |
| 104 | A stage-3 window counts the wall model as validated only when both stored receipts exist and pass: the flat plate against ITTC-57 at Re_L >= 1e6, and the channel against Dean (1978). The worse of the two errors is the one checked. | The builder's first version took the better of the two, so the easy channel case cleared windows while the plate, which is the developing external boundary layer a hull actually sees, failed at -16.5 %. Until the plate passes, every window is ok = False and nothing is refit; the report says why. |
| 105 | The refit pools only the part Stage 1 scales: `hull` for a USV, `body` for the glider and UAS. A USV's wing windows are pooled separately and reported as `wing_not_used`. | Stage 1 has one drag multiplier per vehicle. Pooling hull water drag with wing air drag would mix two different flows into one number. |
| 106 | Sphere-drag receipts with the native LBM are run one grid at a time in tmux on homebase (Re = 1e3 at d = 24 is about 16 h on the loaded box). Re = 1e4 at d = 40 (27.6 M cells, about 132 h on this CPU) needs a GPU, and the subscription's GPU quota is 0, so its row stays FAIL "NOT STORED" with the command to run. | This is a missing physical input (a GPU), not a time limit: on CPU the run takes longer than the box can be dedicated to it. |
| 107 | The DTMB 5415 runner was resumed at 10:18 UTC on 2026-10-29 for Fr 0.20 and 0.10, after the 06:20 clean restart had run only fine Fr 0.41. Fine-grid C_T so far: Fr 0.28 4.060e-3 against 4.23e-3 (-4.0 %), and Fr 0.41 6.812e-3 against 6.67e-3 (+2.1 %). | Of the brief's four speeds, two were still missing. |
| 108 | The flat-plate wall-model receipt is still judged against ITTC-57 at 10 % and fails: the strip mean is C_f 0.003673 against ITTC-57's 0.004398, -16.5 %. For context, over the same strip (Re_x 5.3e4 to 9.8e5) it is -6.4 % against Hughes (1952), 0.066/(log10 Re - 2.03)^2 = 0.003925, and -12.5 % against Schoenherr = 0.004200. | The brief names ITTC-57 for this receipt and gives no tolerance for it. ITTC-57 is a model-ship correlation line set deliberately above Hughes's 2-D flat-plate line, so part of the shortfall is the choice of reference. The tolerance was not relaxed after seeing the result. Whether the gate should use a 2-D friction line is listed as an open question in STATUS.md. |
| 109 | Each vehicle class gets its own emulator, keyed by the `cls` column; a class is trained only once the table holds 100 of its rows. A time shift moves the deploy and leaves the window fixed; a UAS shift moves its sorties through the day. A move shifts deploy, station and waypoints together. Duty scales the glider's dive depth and the UAS's `every_days`. (amended by 535) | The failure modes and features differ by class. A change that leaves the window or the data box is refused as `Invalid`. |
| 110 | The emulator's RMSE acceptance (BRIEF 2.7, <= 0.05) is judged on the held-out RMSE against the true P(fail): the observed RMSE less, in quadrature, the targets' own binomial noise sqrt(mean p(1-p)/n). The observed RMSE and the noise floor are both reported next to it. | Each target is itself an estimate from 16-128 members, with noise of up to about 0.1. Judging the raw RMSE would fail any emulator, including a perfect one. |
| 111 | The flip search tries single changes only, ranked in notches (6 h = 0.5 deg = 25 % duty = one notch), each a different mission (two that make the same mission are one and the cheaper stays, and a UAS duty costs the duty its whole-day period really is). (amended by 733) It asks the emulator first. For candidates the emulator cannot answer, it runs Stage 1 on the 3 cheapest, one batch each, on the base run's member ids (common random numbers), until the share runs out. `--drop` needs no simulation because Stage-1 vehicles do not interact. | This keeps the search inside the emulator's 5 % share (20 % when nothing was refit), and every Stage-1 comparison shares random numbers with the base run. |
| 112 | The training table `$CAFE_DATA/emulator/train.parquet` is not a ledger input. Its path and row count go in a note. Rows are keyed so that a rerun replaces its own rows; the read-modify-write is not locked. | Later runs change the table, which would break the audit's hash check. Concurrent runs can lose rows; that is acceptable for one box running one pipeline at a time. |
| 113 | The reach map runs Dijkstra (`cafe.reach._dijkstra`) on a 41 x 41 grid over the currents at deploy time; gliders use the 5-100 m mean. Through-water speeds are assumptions: glider 0.23 m/s, USV 1.4 m/s, 24 h horizon. Water outside the ingested data counts as unknown. | The UAS is not drawn and the reason is given, because its range is set by its battery and its ship, not by currents. |
| 114 | A sensitivity (`sensitivity.dominant`, the report's "most sensitive to" and top-3) is named only when its EASI S1 clears a permutation noise floor: the 95th percentile of max-over-parameters S1 across 200 seeded shuffles of the failure indicator (`stats.sensitivity.noise_floor`). The floor is stored and reported next to the S1 values. All S1 values are still stored. (amended by 277) | In the quick rehearsal on the real mission (32 members), glider_eq showed S1 0.28 for `usv_vpp_bias` and the UAS 0.30 for `glider_speed_scale`, parameters those vehicles never read. At this size EASI's noise reaches about 0.3. A family-wise permutation test is the smallest change that keeps such noise out of the report and still names a real driver. |
| 115 | mypy runs with `strict = true` (pyproject), and the audit's mypy step reads that config. `# type: ignore` is used only on third-party calls whose typing is defective (warp, trimesh, jax.config, smt), each with its error code and a reason. | BRIEF exit condition: mypy --strict. Ignores on this repo's own code would hide real errors. |

| 135 | P(fail) between two independent runs is judged by Newcombe's (1998, Stat. Med. 17:873, method 10) hybrid Wilson-score interval for p1 - p2, which must contain 0 at family-wise 95 % (Bonferroni: each of the m vehicles and links compared at 1 - 0.05 / m). `intervals.newcombe_diff`; the old "new estimate inside the first run's 90 % interval" test is gone. (amended by 596) | A new estimate inside the interval of an independent estimate fails about 25 % of the time even when the code is perfectly reproducible, because the first run's interval ignores the second run's own noise. Bonferroni is conservative, which is the safe side for a gate that should only fail on a real change; 0/32 against 16/32 still fails at m = 100. |
| 136 | Nothing that decides how much work a stage does is read from the clock. `budget.stage3_plan` (LES cell-steps per part, the FEA go/no-go, the free-surface cases that fit) and `budget.emulator_plan` (the number of variant runs) compute every size from the plan, `config/throughput.json` and `config/assumptions.yaml` only. `Context` writes them into `plan.json` (`stage3`, `emulator`); the stages recompute them from `ctx.plan` with the same functions. A result that depends on how busy the machine was cannot be reproduced, which BRIEF 5.3 requires. |
| 137 | A stage-3 LES part gets `rate x (slice - FEA reserve - stage3.compile_s_guess) / stage3.les_margin` cell-steps, where slice = the stage share / the number of LES parts (`STAGE3_PARTS` x windows per vehicle) and the FEA reserve (`stage3.fea_reserve_frac` of a slice) applies to gliders only. `stage3.les_margin` = 1.5 is new. The compile time is a plan input and is never replaced by a measurement. A slice below `stage3.min_window_s`, or an FEA reserve below `stage3.fea_min_s`, is decided at plan time and reads "not run" / "not planned"; `stage3.fea_min_s` is 68.2 s since round 13, the measured static solve plus the buckling step, not the 8 s of a static solve on a coarse mesh. (amended by 741) | The old code measured the compile and the LBM rate on the first window and sized the later ones from them. The assumption is in `config/assumptions.yaml` with its `replace_with`. |
| 138 | The safety kills: the LES run stops after the chunk that spends its slice (`windows.wall_hook`: no projection, no shortening of a run that is on time), the FEA child (its whole process group, ccx included, since round 13: amended by 734) and the free-surface child are killed at the slice or stage deadline, and a window whose turn comes with less than `stage3.min_window_s` left is not started. Each gives `ok` False and a first reason beginning "killed". A killed LES row keeps its partial coefficients but is never `ok`, whatever its checks say, so the refit cannot use it. | 139 | Timings leave the hashed outputs. The only measured quantity left in a hashed file is `query_ms` in `emulator/holdout.json` (BRIEF 2.7 asks for a query time). | The report's receipts carry the sha256 of these files, so a timing inside them made every receipt differ between identical runs and forced `acceptance.normalize` to drop whole stages. | 140 | The emulator trains on `emulator/train_input.parquet`: the training table as the emulator stage found it, minus the rows this run (its `run_id`) wrote before, plus this run's rows and its variants' rows. The `Emulator` passes its seed to SMT (`seed`), which otherwise draws its random starts from OS entropy. | BRIEF 2.7 says every run appends to the table, so a later run legitimately learns from an earlier one. What must not happen is the table changing the emulator of a run that has already started, or an unrecorded input. A snapshot hashed in the ledger makes the dependence explicit. A `run_id` derived from the configuration rather than the run directory would let identical runs share one table, but it would also merge runs whose cached data differ; a private copy per run is the smaller change. |

Skipping keeps the number of variants a function of the plan. |

| 146 | Mutation testing is mutmut 3.8.1 on `cafe/pm/physics` and `cafe/pm/stats` (`[tool.mutmut]` in pyproject.toml; the whole `cafe` package is the source path and `only_mutate` selects the two trees). The score is killed / total over every mutant mutmut generates: survivors, timeouts, mutants no test reaches and suspicious ones all count against it, and the gate is 0.85. The tests run against each mutant are `pytest_add_cli_args_test_selection` (the physics and stats test files). | BRIEF 5.3 asks for >= 85 % killed. Counting only `killed` in the numerator is the conservative reading: a timeout may be a real kill, but it may also be a hung worker, so it is not claimed. Selecting the test files by name, not running the whole suite, is what keeps a run to hours. |
| 147 | `make mutation` runs mutmut (`--max-children $(MUT_CPUS)`, 3), exports its counts and writes `docs/mutation.json`: killed, survived, timeout, no_tests, suspicious, skipped, segfault, total, score, a sha256 of the mutated code and of the tests that kill its mutants (`audit.mutation_digest`), the test list, date and mutmut version. `make audit` fails if that file is missing, was made by different physics/stats code or tests, or scores < 0.85 (recomputed from the counts, never read from `score`), and the message names the command that refreshes it. `docs/mutation.json` is committed. | The same slow-receipt pattern as `receipts --slow`: a run takes hours, so `audit` only reads a stored result and refuses a stale one. The file is in docs/, not config/, because `pipeline.code_digest` hashes config/ and a result file there would invalidate every stored receipt. The digest covers the mutated files, the listed tests and tests/conftest.py, not the rest of the tree: an edit elsewhere cannot change which of these mutants the tests kill. |

| 150 | Mutants that are equivalent are left as survivors, not pragma'd, except where a whole class would be hidden: `cast(None, x)` for `cast(F, x)`, `int.from_bytes(b)` without "big" (the default since Python 3.11), `generate_state(2, ...)[0]` for `generate_state(1, ...)[0]` (prefix-stable), `jax` indexing past a size-1 array (clamped), `np.asarray(pwm[:5])[map]` for `[:4]` with a map of indices < 4, and a probability-zero equality (`>=` for `>` on a continuous bound). | A survivor that cannot be killed costs 1 / total of the score; a per-line pragma would also silence the other mutants on that line, and many are the main formula of a function. | 183 | The USV speed gap is shown and diagnosed, and the prior is not changed (the diagnosis found no model fault). Stage 1 stores per USV member the mean speed over ground (path length / time since deploy), the mean 10 m wind and the wind at the anemometer height along the track, and their ratios (`vehicles[usv].speed`: `sog_ms`, `u10_ms`, `wind_anem_ms`, `ratio`, `ratio_anem`, their medians and `calibration_ratio` = 0.96 / 5.4); the report's USV block and the sanity row print the ratio beside Chiodi's 0.178 with 'the calibration scenario differs from the mission (no current, uniform wind, sailing to a station)' and with the finding: the mission ratio is lowered by the westward current, and the calibration may already include part of the current effect, a possible double penalty that is not established. Limitation L18 and QUESTIONS_FOR_LAB 14 carry the question (default: assume the 0.178 does not include currents; the prior stays as calibrated). (amended by 467) | Review round 2, M-2. In q2 the mean path speed per member is 0.41 to 0.98 m/s (mean 0.627) against a mean U10 of 5.86 m/s along the track, a ratio of 0.10 to 0.12, and 13 of 16 members are `swept_off_track`. The 0.178 comes from 48 vehicles sailing to a station from 6 degrees away in uniform wind with no current for 12 h. Diagnosis (batch B, the real model along the mission, one CPU): nominal ratio 0.120 (0.125 at the anemometer height). Mission wind alone 0.186, plus waves 0.180, plus current 0.150, all three 0.120. The current costs a third, all of it from the zonal part (the westward South Equatorial Current, mean |c| 0.33 m/s): a beam reach with a leeward set becomes close-hauled (true wind angle 85 to 45 deg) and the speed through the water falls from 1.1 to 0.72 m/s. Track holding costs little (0.127 opened against 0.120). The current is counted once. The open question is on the data side: Chiodi's 0.178 was observed in real currents and the calibration scenario has none, so the current may be penalised twice. Row 163 stands. |

| 211 | The "most sensitive to" entry of a vehicle or link is the first-order EASI index (row 114) of one continuous margin per member, over the columns that can act on its class. Margins: UAS, the lowest charge above the reserve over its flown sorties; station glider and station USV, the largest distance from the station; section USV, the largest cross-track error; section or drift glider, the battery energy left above the reserve; link, the fraction of the required moments that worked. Acting columns: the six forcing errors, plus `glider_speed_scale` and `battery_capacity_scale` (glider), `usv_vpp_bias` (USV), `bulk_stress_scale` and `battery_capacity_scale` (UAS); a link takes the union over its ends. The 0/1 failure indicator is the fallback (no margin, or fewer than 16 finite margins); on it, with fewer than 3 failures or 3 passes, no index is computed and the entry prints the hypercube ranks of the minority's members for the three most extreme parameters (`stats.sensitivity.extreme_ranks`). Each entry carries `case` (ranked, nothing_above_floor, too_few, all_pass, all_fail), `quantity` and a finished `text`; the report prints that sentence, with the exact S1 when a parameter is named, and a summary without `text` keeps the old wording. This ranks one outcome, not the verdict: a UAS can fail by wind or attitude while the ranked margin is its battery. Row 114 is amended. (Amended in review round 4, see 253: the indices rank only the sampled columns, and the report says so.) (amended by 277) | Review round 3, M-2. EASI on a 0/1 vector with k ones saturates at its own permutation floor (n = 32, ten uniform columns, failures the k lowest values of one column: the driver was found in 0, 0, 16 and 20 of 20 draws at k = 1, 2, 3, 6), so the demo named no parameter for the UAS and the USV; columns that cannot act (`glider_speed_scale` on a UAS) set the floor; and "no parameter clears the noise floor" read as "none matters". |

| 238 | The derived surface layer is built from a key (sha256 of the orca manifest, the ocean store's manifest hash, the GEBCO cut and the code digest) stored in its attributes and in `derived.digest.json` beside it. Ingest records the store hash as a ledger input and `ingest/derived.digest.json` as an output; stage 1, windows, 1b and the emulator fail the check `derived_unchanged` if the store no longer hashes to it; `audit` re-hashes the store. A store whose hours do not span the window is refused (`ConfigError` naming the window and the span), not clipped by `Axis.locate`. Hashing the demo layer (218 MB, 567 files) takes about a second per use. The orca manifest the key hashes lists the parts of its window only, not every file in `hours/` (round 12, M-29). (amended by 342, 396 and 669) | Review round 4, M-2. The store that every Stage 1, 1b and UAS result reads was in no hash, check or invalidation rule: in the q4 run it was 2.4 days older than the orca cache beside it, and an edited store passed the ledger. |

| 244 | The two-ray RF loss is computed over a 4/3-radius earth (Kerr's specular point, tangent-plane heights for the path lengths and the grazing angle, the divergence factor D of the spherical surface on the reflected field, with the Ament factor). While the grazing angle at the specular point is above psi_c = (lambda / pi a)^(1/3) (2.3 mrad at 915 MHz, **assumed**: recalled, not re-read) that is the loss; from there to the 4/3-earth radio horizon the loss is the larger of it and the plane-earth loss; beyond the horizon the curved loss as before (no link). Inside 2 km it equals the plane-earth result to 0.15 dB; for 15 m / 3 m masts the limit is at 6.9 km of a 23.1 km horizon and `rf_margin(20 km)` is +13.0 dB. The d^-4 receipt uses the plane earth (`a_eff` = 1e12 m). The method string says "two-ray over a 4/3-radius earth with divergence (Kerr)". Amends row 65. (amended by 652) | Review round 4, M-8, status partly. The finding's table (curved, D = 1: 9 to 40 dB more loss than flat) is reproduced to 0.1 dB, but with D, which its own fix requires, the sign reverses: the flat model was pessimistic beyond 10 km (curved with D falls toward free space, 120.9 dB at 22 km against 140.6 flat). Geometric optics fails just inside the horizon and diffraction (ITU-R P.526) is not modelled, so the flat loss stands in there: conservative by construction. No demo number changes (the only RF link is 89 % beyond the horizon; the UAS link is inside 2 km). psi_c is a number in code, not in a yaml, so it is not in the credibility table. |

The finding said one 50 ms sample; the hook is called once per record interval (1 s). A masked Hs is not a calm sea: 0 is the deepest null. |

| 266 | Stage 1 runs on every CPU the run may use (`cafe/pm/par.py`): `CAFE_WORKERS` when set, else the affinity mask cut to the cgroup quota (`docker --cpus`). Inside a batch each glider and each USV is a task and each UAS sortie is one (`stage1._vehicle`, `stage1._uas_sortie`); a sortie draws only its own turbulence stream (`_uas_forcing(only=i)`) and the gust fronts of the whole window, so its streams, seeds and 3-D wind counts are the one-process run's. When a batch does not fill the workers `stage1.run` runs a wave of batches from threads (`stage1.wave`: what n_min still needs, or workers // tasks per batch, never past n_max), merges them in member order and stops where the one-process run stops; a batch run past the stop is dropped. `pipeline.run` opens one pool for every stage, so stage 1b, the emulator variants and the fresh draws use it too and each worker starts and compiles once. Workers are spawned, never forked; a task inside a worker runs inline. Results carry no `sim` objects any more (nothing read them; the pipeline already dropped them before `res.pkl`). (amended by 281 and 377) | The 1-CPU profile of one 32-member q4 batch (300 s under cProfile): glider_eq 53 s, glider_2n 54 s, USV 77 s, UAS 115 s, and 58 % of it in `env/grid.py:interp`, numpy calls on 32-element arrays that hold the GIL, so threads cannot run it in parallel and processes must. A fork of a process with live XLA threads hangs (row 149). Measured on the q4 environment, 2 CPUs of a shared box: one worker 225.7 s, two workers 109.7 s, the result equal bit for bit (`tests/test_par.py` checks the same on the ci environment, on two workers, through a two-batch wave). |
| 267 | The stage-1 workers read the environment from shared memory: every array of 1 MiB or more travels as a read-only memory map of an .npy file written once per process, in /dev/shm when it has 12 GiB free and in the temporary directory otherwise, and `par.adopt` swaps the parent's own arrays for those maps when it runs more than one worker (`pipeline.Context.env`), so the environment is in memory once. `Ocean.__getstate__` reads every HYCOM field before it is sent. `pm.sh`, `bin/pmx.sh` and the RUNBOOK's `pm()` pass `--shm-size 16g` (`PM_SHM`). | The q4 environment is 2.9 GB of WRF and UMWM plus 1.2 GB per HYCOM field read (about 9 GB with the five), so a copy per worker would not fit 8 workers in the 28 GB container, and Docker's default /dev/shm is 64 MB. Before `Ocean.__getstate__`, each task read the HYCOM fields again from Zarr into its own memory (a worker peaked at 7.2 GB, and every glider task paid the read); after it, the parent holds 1.3 GB once the environment is adopted. |
| 268 | A task runs with the parent's module state of `par.STATE` (the run's sub-grid calibration `subgrid._RUN_CAL`, the mission's verdict cut-offs in `stats.intervals`), sent with each task. The environment pickles: the bathymetry sigma callables are `functools.partial`s of module functions instead of lambdas, `AnalyticEnv`'s default current profile is a module function, and `PerturbedEnv.__getattr__` refuses `env` itself (unpickling asked for it before `__dict__` was filled and recursed). A worker that dies in native code prints its traceback (`faulthandler`). `res.pkl` is written without the pickle memo (`pipeline.res_bytes`). | A spawned worker starts from the shipped calibration and the default cut-offs: stage 1b with the run's own calibration would have run its tasks on the shipped one. Each change is the smallest that lets the objects stage 1 already passes around cross a process boundary unchanged. A vehicle that comes back from a worker carries its own copies of the key strings and constants a one-process run shares, so with the memo the ci run's res.pkl hashed differently on two workers with every value equal, and the report's receipts (and so the same-seed check) differed; without it the ci run gives the same res.pkl sha-256 and the same normalized report.json on one and two workers. |
| 269 | `env.grid` is faster with the same bits: `Axis` keeps its spacings, `locate` clamps with `np.minimum`/`np.maximum` (what `np.clip` returns, NaN included), the corner weights are written in place of `np.stack`, the strides are integer products, `broadcast_arrays` runs only when the queries differ in shape, and `interp` takes leading dimensions of the field (each slice interpolated exactly as on its own), so `GriddedEnv._wind3d` reads every level of Z, U and V in three calls where it made one call per level. | The profile of a q4 batch spent 58 % in `interp`, nearly all per-call overhead on 32-point queries (`np.clip` alone about 31 s of 300). Results robustness 2 counts first-order sensitivities only when the number ranked is improbable by luck over the report's tests (with m permutation tests and k ranked parameters, P(Binomial(m, 0.05) >= k) < 5 %), else the evidence says "none stands clearly above chance"; a refit alone still earns level 2, and the printed rule says so. A summary without `noise_floor` counts as clear. (amended by 413 and 437) | Review round 7, M-23. Amends the text of row 256. "Lint checked" was stronger than "configured" (the audit does not run ruff), and q7's level 2 rested on one sensitivity 0.006 above a floor that one test in twenty clears, among three tests (1 of 3 cleared the 95 % floor: a 14 % chance by luck). The finding wanted a stricter second floor; the summary stores only the 95 % one, so the count is used (a second floor would be a stage-1 change). q8 measures the Robustness evidence level. |

The bounds are `ingest.*` entries of `config/assumptions.yaml`, each with a unit, a `why` ("Gross-error bound, not a model of the weather") and a `replace_with`. A wind field 3 times too strong is inside 100 m/s and passes the range check; the u* fraction (0.0 %) and the Data pedigree rule are what catch it. | 525 | The reach polygon's sector of a cell comes from its bearing rounded to 1e-9 degree BEFORE the `% 360`: `sec = (np.round(np.degrees(np.arctan2(EE, NN)), 9) % 360.0) // (360.0 / nb)` (`report._reach_one`). `th`, the bearing the vertex is drawn at, stays the unrounded one. Rounding after the `% 360` would give exactly 360.0 and sector 24, which no sector holds. No tie-break is added for the farthest cell: in the 41 x 41 grid no two cells of one sector have the same N^2 + E^2 (checked over every integer offset), so `np.argmax` on the radius has no tie to break. `ok = tt <= hours * 3600` is a real threshold and is unchanged. | A cell exactly on a diagonal or an axis has a bearing that is a multiple of 15 degrees, and `ang // 15` flipped on the last bit of `arctan2` (135.00000000000003 is sector 9, 134.99999999999997 sector 8): the same cell, one ulp apart, gave another farthest cell, so the glider_eq corners differed by 0.048 degree between burst's CPU and orca's (P-3), with no verdict changed.
