| 535 | An emulator is trained on, and answers, one scope: the vehicle class, the platform and the length of the window (`budget.emulator_scope`, for example `glider/seaglider/144h`; a `scope` column in the training table). `whatif._clean` keeps only the rows of the class and scope, `MIN_ROWS` counts distinct feature points per scope, `train` returns one emulator (or none, with its reason) per scope, `usable` and the queries (`flip_search`, `answer`, the CLI `whatif`) look up their own scope only (an emulator of another scope is no emulator, so the answer comes from Stage 1), and the row key includes the scope. The plan counts points per scope (`plan.json` `vehicle_scopes` and `points_per_scope`, replacing `points_per_class`; a plan made before counts per class), `holdout.json` entries carry `scope`, and the ledger checks are named `emulator_training_points_<scope>` and `emulator_range_guard_<scope>`. A table made before the column has no scope on its rows: they are never trained on and never rewritten, are counted as `unscoped_rows` and are named in the refusal. No feature was added. (amended by 583) | Review round 10, M-1. P(fail) is over the whole window and the features (BASE and TASK) hold neither the window length nor the platform, so a 3-day and a 6-day mission with the same inputs were pooled as one function (the reviewer's q10 figures: P(fail) 0.156 and 0.234), and RUNBOOK step 5 told users to add points by running other missions. A variant never changes the window or the platform, so a feature would be a constant column and degenerate the GP and its z-scoring; a scope refuses to pool what is not one function. The price is slower accumulation: points are counted per platform and per window length (the demo's two gliders are two scopes of one point per variant each; a lab that mixes 3-day and 6-day missions needs 100 points of each). Amends 109 (keyed by class only), 174 (the row key and `MIN_ROWS`), 188 (`points_per_class` is `points_per_scope`), 261 (what makes the table grow), 264 (a scope whose emulator missed a gate is not used) and 531 (the smallest scope). Not changed: `key_cols` still omits the mission name, so two missions of one scope with the same deploy offset, position and task count as one point in `MIN_ROWS` and fall on one side of the hold-out; that undercounts and never leaks (the weather columns are features, so the GP still sees both rows), and adding the name would raise the count from near-identical reruns, which is the owner's choice. Every row made before this change is stale by `code_digest` anyway. Tests (each fails on the pooled code): `tests/test_whatif.py::test_the_scope_is_the_class_the_platform_and_the_length_of_the_window_and_a_variant_keeps_it`, `::test_rows_of_another_window_length_are_not_pooled_and_the_refusal_says_so`, `::test_train_fits_one_emulator_per_scope_and_a_query_is_answered_from_its_own_only`, `::test_a_table_made_before_the_scope_column_is_left_out_and_says_so_instead_of_failing`, `tests/test_budget.py::test_the_emulator_plan_counts_points_per_scope_so_two_platforms_of_one_class_do_not_add_up`, `tests/test_report.py::test_r10_m1_a_holdout_file_without_scopes_still_reads_per_class_and_a_plan_without_scopes_counts_per_class`. |
| 536 | BRIEF 5.5's "force time-series converged: running mean within 2 % over the last third" is read as "the mean has converged", not "every sample is within 2 % of the mean". `nest.running_mean_ok` takes the last third of the series, splits it into `RUNNING_MEAN_BLOCKS` = 5 batch means (`np.array_split`, the construction and count `windows.coefficients` already uses) and takes the running mean of those five values against the last-third mean. The limit 0.02 and the `(ok, dev)` shape are unchanged; `docs/BRIEF.md` is the owner's spec and is not edited. | Review round 10, M-2. The sample running mean restarted at the first sample of the last third, so its deviation was at least that sample's own noise, which does not shrink with run length: white noise of 5 % failed 4 seeds in 5 (seed 0 dev 0.0401) though the third's mean is known to 0.2 %, and 2 % noise 2 in 5, with the untrue reason "no convergence". Now 5 % noise (n 3000, seeds 0 to 4) passes (largest dev 0.006), AR(1) with rho 0.95 and a 2 % marginal sd passes (largest 0.0145), and a ramp across the last third from 1 to 1.06 fails (0.0233). Limits, stated: the check is less strict by design (a 5 % ramp gave 0.0244 and failed, now 0.0195 and passes; a 6 % ramp still fails); AR(1) at rho 0.95 with a 5 % marginal sd fails any 2 % test of the mean over a third of 3000 samples (dev 0.004 to 0.036 over seeds), so a stricter limit or a burn-in is the owner's decision; real LBM drag was not measured (q10 has no LBM rows). Consequence: windows with ordinary force fluctuation now pass this row and can reach the refit, so a GPU run's refit and golden can differ from one made before. Caller: `windows.stage3_checks` only. Test: `tests/test_nest.py::test_r10_m2_the_running_mean_judges_the_mean_not_one_sample`. |

| 544 | `wing_stall_angle` is counted only while the apparent wind speed is at or above `trim_min_aws_ms` (1.5 m/s): `(awa < min_awa_deg) & (aws >= trim_min_aws_ms)` in `USVSim.step`. Below it only `wing_stall_calm` counts, so the two modes are exclusive per step and a calm is only a calm. The solver and the polar are not changed. | Review round 10, M-10 (A-6). At zero wind the table's apparent wind angle was [180, 0, 0] for headings 0, 90, 180 against the direct solver's [180, 180, 180]: the polar's tws = 0 row takes its angle from the sign of a zero and `_lin` blends it into the whole band 0 < tws < 1. A sweep found 6123 cells where the table is inside the bow cone and the direct solver is not, all with tws at most 0.99 and aws at most 1.06, below `trim_min_aws_ms`, where `wing_stall_calm` also holds (both holds 1800 s, 217), so verdicts and first-failure times are unchanged and only the mode count was wrong. The demo after the change (against q10, same commit, orca): verdicts and `first_fail_t` identical, `wing_stall_calm` 30, `wing_stall_angle` 14 to 12, `becalmed` 22, `swept_off_track` 54, `capsize_proxy` 0. Side effect, stated: the angle clock now resets while aws is under 1.5 m/s, so a member that spends 900 s in a calm with the nose inside the cone and then 900 s in the cone above 1.5 m/s no longer reaches the 1800 s hold as `wing_stall_angle`; the shipped holds are equal and the demo shows no change. Amends 217 (the angle clause is conditional on the wind speed). The `why` strings of `min_awa_deg` and `trim_min_aws_ms` in `platforms/saildrone_explorer.yaml` still describe the clauses without this condition and were not edited. Test: `tests/test_usv.py::test_r10_m10_in_a_calm_the_bow_cone_is_not_counted_so_wing_stall_angle_needs_an_apparent_wind_of_trim_min_aws_ms` (0.5 m/s toward 210 degrees and 0.3 m/s toward 180 degrees: the defect depends on the bearing, so the winds are the ones that reproduce it; a 6 m/s wind with `min_awa_deg` 180 still gives `wing_stall_angle`). |

| 546 | `acceptance` removes only what it made itself. `acceptance.claim_out(out, names)` marks `--out` with `.cafe_acceptance` before the first removal. An unmarked `--out` is a `ConfigError` (exit 2, nothing touched, no marker written) when it holds `<name>`, `<name>_fetch`, `<name>.log` or `<name>_fetch.log` for a run name (quick, quick_same, quick_seed, overnight, 4h), or is itself a run (`plan.json` or `checkpoints/`). An unmarked `--out` that holds one of `OWN_FILES` (`acceptance.json`, `acceptance.txt`, `emulator_table.parquet`, `receipts_broken.json`, `receipts_wave_direction.json`, `receipts_broken.log`, the names only `run` writes there) and is not itself a run is an acceptance directory from before the marker: it is marked and replaced as before. A missing or empty directory, or one with only other files, is claimed and marked (the other files stay); with the marker present the runs are replaced on every run. `run` checks every run name before the first run starts, `run_one` checks its own two directories and logs, and the marker is written before the first `rmtree`, so a run killed half-way is rerunnable. The `--out` help text names the refusal. | Review round 10, M-12 (B-3). `acceptance --out runs` removed the user's reviewed `runs/quick` and `runs/overnight` (a 10 h result) with no prompt and overwrote `<name>.log`; 328 answered it only for `make acceptance`, whose Makefile points at `runs/acceptance`. The first version refused a `runs/acceptance` made before the marker, which would have stopped the next 11 h `make acceptance`; the `OWN_FILES` rule fixed that at `claim_out`, the one place `run` and `run_one` both pass. Residual, stated: a user directory that holds a file with an `OWN_FILES` name plus a `quick` is claimed; a user's own `quick` added to a directory acceptance already marked is replaced (the marker means acceptance's); a run killed before `emulator_table.parquet` was written (only `quick/` and `quick.log`) is still refused and the message says what to do. Other deletes of a user-named directory: only the two `acceptance` sites (searched `rmtree`, `unlink`, `os.remove`, `shutil.move` in `cafe/pm`). Amends 328; `refuse_wiped_mission` (round 9 M-12) still runs first. Tests: `tests/test_acceptance.py::test_r10_m12_*` (refusals, marking, rerun, a killed run, the mission refusal first, an earlier acceptance directory claimed, a directory that is itself a run refused). |

| 557 | `bin/pm_checks.sh` is the one place that decides what `pm.sh`, `bin/pmx.sh` and `bin/orca_ready.sh` (and, for tags, `bin/orca_gate.sh` and `bin/proot_suite.sh`) may paste into a command line; each rule exits 2 before anything is sent. `PM_CPUS`, `PM_THREADS` and `CAFE_MIN_FREE_GB` (proot) are whole numbers from 1 (`02`, `1.5`, `2,` and `2;x` are refused); `PM_MEM` and `PM_SHM` (docker) are such a number with an optional `k`, `m`, `g`, `kb`, `mb`, `gb` or `b` (upper case too); `PM_STOP_GRACE` stays whole seconds (0 allowed); `PMX` stays `pm_` and a plain name; `TM` is 2 or more letters, digits, `_` or `-`, the first a letter or digit. `bin/proot_run.sh` refuses a `PM_CPUS` that is not a whole number from 1 too (exit 2, before the proot files), for a run by hand on the worker. A decimal `PM_CPUS` such as 1.5 is no longer accepted (a search of the Makefile, bench, docs, HANDOFF, CLAUDE.md, CI and the scripts found no caller). Add a new variable pasted into a runner command line to `pm_job`, not to the scripts. (amended by 610) | Review round 10, M-23 (D-10). The value is parsed by a shell on the worker or by docker, so a `;` was a command (`PM_CPUS='2;x'` ran `x` on orca, `CAFE_MIN_FREE_GB='1;echo INJ'` ran `echo INJ`), `2,` became `CAFE_WORKERS=2,` with a misleading 32-CPU warning, `TM=x` is no docker name and `TM=-x` is a tmux option. Only the caller's own environment could inject, so the harm was a wrong run, not an attack; `PM_STOP_GRACE` was checked and the others were not. `orca_sync.sh` quotes `CAFE_MIN_FREE_GB` through `q()`, and `orca_setup.sh` and `orca_watch.sh` read it in their own shell, so they need no edit. The docker path was not run (no docker on orca, homebase off limits); the docker rule is from docker's documented name pattern. Amends 488 (TM: also 2 or more characters, first a letter or digit), 500 (the `PM_STOP_GRACE` check moved here, same rule and wording) and 524 (`PM_THREADS` is validated on the laptop as well). Tests: `tests/test_runners.py::test_m23_*` (what was valid is still sent, in both runtimes and both scripts). |

The writer `_save` cannot produce the shape (hand damage or schema drift). Completes and amends 452. | 560 | `bin/orca_watch.sh` writes its disk note once per dip: it is latched while free space is under `FLOOR + 40` GB and cleared when it reaches `FLOOR + 45` (5 GB of hysteresis, so a value hovering at the line writes no snap a minute onto a nearly full disk). The first look after the upgrade writes one extra note per failing finished log (the old state files carry no mtime); passing logs write none. Anything that touches a finished failing log is a new state and notes again. `stat -c %Y` is GNU; the recorder runs only on orca. Amends 522. | 561 | `report.check_rendered` asks more than that a node's text is somewhere on the page: `report._n` tags the first anchor of every number span with `data-path` (the path or paths of the node in `report.json`, filled by `render` from `_walk(rep)`), and for each path shape the page prints for any vehicle, link or window (`vehicles.*.modes.battery`; `INSTANCE` stands for the id after `vehicles`, `links`, `windows`, `forcing_at_windows`, `refit` and for `[n]`) every node of that shape must be printed under its own path with its own text. A shape the page prints nowhere is not asked for, and nodes that are in `report.json` only (`mission.box`, `map.vehicles.*.deploy`, the `sensitivity_scope.*` nodes written into a sentence) are not asked for. A page without `data-path` is judged by the whole-token rule of 175 alone. (amended by 585) | Review round 10, M-27 (D-15). Every node was searched as a whole token in the whole page, so a section that stopped printing "0 members", "64 members" or "1" still passed (q10: 535 number nodes, 273 distinct texts, "0" x254, "1" x163, "64 members" x30; removing one rendered "0" gave 0 problems), and 533 was this defect seen once. A count rule (spans with the text at least nodes with the text) was tried and is too weak, since the page prints some nodes twice (64 members: 13 nodes, 30 spans); "every node is a span" failed a dozen report tests and would fail the report stage on any run with a flip search. With the rule, removing the text of one single-path span of q10 is found in each case. `index.html` bytes change (the attribute), so its sha-256 changes; no golden holds them (`acceptance.py` reads it for `cpu_fallback` text only). Open: a node printed only for some instances of a shape would be a false failure; none exists in q10 or the fixtures, and the fix would be to give that shape its own path. A report whose whole section stops printing is still caught only by the old rule. Amends 175 and 533 (the 73-members case is now caught). Tests: `tests/test_report.py::test_r10_m27_a_card_that_stops_printing_a_number_the_others_print_is_found_though_the_text_is_on_the_page`, `::test_r10_m27_every_number_node_of_a_rendered_report_is_printed_under_its_own_path_where_its_shape_is_printed`; `test_check_rendered_matches_whole_numeric_tokens` is unchanged. |

| 603 | `lbm.bounce_back` divides the moving-wall term by 2q on every link with q >= 1/2 (`wall = where(q >= 0.5, 1/(2q), 1)`), as Bouzidi, Firdaouss and Lallemand (2001) divide the whole q >= 1/2 expression: `f_qbar = [f_q+(x) - 6 w rho c.u_w]/(2q) + (2q-1)/(2q) f_qbar+(x)`. Links with q < 1/2 and the scalar halfway path are untouched (the halfway results are bit-identical). `qdist` is the halfway wall only when it is the number 0.5 (`isinstance(qdist, (int, float)) and qdist == 0.5`); any other scalar is that wall distance on every link, and an array is as before. A sphere carried by the stream (`nest.uniform_flow_force(bouzidi=True)`, 40x28x28, r 5, 400 steps) has a maximum of the force over (rho U^2 d^2) of 7.78 before and 4.1e-6 after; the halfway wall's mean over the last 100 steps, 8.6e-6, is unchanged. Slow receipts: the stored `nest_sphere_*` grids are static-wall runs, so their Cd cannot change; they are stale only because `lbm.physics_sha()` changed, and whoever restores them expects the same Cd to rounding. (amends 76) | Review round 11, M-11 (C-1). For q >= 1/2 the code subtracted the halfway wall's `6 w rho (c.u)` unscaled, so a body whose velocity equals the flow's saw a force: the reviewer's mean 0.389 (batch B measured 0.447 for the mean of the norm over the last 100 steps of the same run, the other average) against 2.8e-6 with the factor. The only path that combines a curved `qdist` with a moving wall is `nest.step` (`nest.py:206`); `qdist` is an array only from `nest.sphere_drag` (a static wall, `u_wall` 0) and the tests, and `stack_geoms` pads halfway bodies with q = 0.5, where the factor is 1, so no printed number or gate moves: the 350 fast receipts, before (3ce8e09) and after, moved no value or verdict (30 FAIL in both). `bounce_back` chose its wall by the Python type of `qdist` (the adjacent weakness the verifier noted): a float 0.3 silently became 0.5 and an int 1 was a correct q = 1; the fix is by value. The new keyword `bouzidi` of `uniform_flow_force` defaults to False, so `nest.receipts()` and its row are unchanged; a second receipt row for the curved case was not added (it would change the receipt count and STATUS): the lead's call. Tests: `tests/test_lbm.py::test_a_curved_wall_carried_by_the_fluid_*`, `::test_a_scalar_wall_distance_other_than_one_half_*`, `tests/test_nest.py::test_uniform_flow_gives_zero_force_on_a_curved_bouzidi_sphere_too`. PHYSICS 14.1 has the formula. |

| 610 | `bin/pm_checks.sh` also decides what a machine's address, a worker's login and a key path may be: `pm_host` (letters, digits, `.`, `-` and `:`; not empty; not starting with `-`: an IPv4 or IPv6 address or a DNS name), `pm_user` (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`; not empty; not starting with `-`) and `pm_key` (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, `/` and `~`, optionally led by the literal `$HOME/`, the default's form; not empty; not starting with `-`). `bin/orca_sync.sh` checks its IP with `pm_host` right after the usage line, before the part-name loop and the first ssh; `bin/worker_setup.sh` requires its first argument and checks the user part of `user@host` with `pm_user`, the host with `pm_host` and the key with `pm_key`; each exits 2 before the first ssh. A name without `@` is still accepted (an ssh config name). `PM_HOST`, `ORCA_VIA` and `PM_HOME_HOST` stay unchecked: they are the caller's own local ssh destination, passed as one argument and never pasted into a remote shell. A re-run of `orca_sync.sh` with no part named copies nothing once `pmdata.done` exists, so a `tao/s2` fetched on homebase after the first copy reaches orca only by naming `pmdata` (all six directories again, about 13 GB, to move a few hundred MB) or by copying `tao` by hand. (amends 496, 557 and 571) | Review round 11, M-18 (B-7). The address was the one caller-typed value pasted unquoted into a command line without a check (part names had been checked before, 496, the PM_* variables since 557): with a stub ssh, `sh bin/orca_sync.sh 'x;id' root` reached the homebase ssh with DST `ssh -p 2222 -i .ssh/burst_ed25519 intern@x;id`, and `1.2.3.4 id` made `id` the remote command on orca through the unquoted `$D '...'` expansion; the caller's own argument, so a wrong run and not an attack. The sibling `worker_setup.sh` pastes its first argument, and (found by the second pass) its second, the key path, into the script homebase runs: `'$(touch /path/pwned)'` there ran on homebase. A host name with `_` and an IPv6 zone id (`fe80::1%eth0`) are refused (not used here: tailnet IPs and `*.ts.net`). The `tao/s2` behaviour is the as-built consequence of 489 and 571 (one marker per part, set by the first whole copy), recorded so that ORCA_RUNNER's "copies the whole tao directory" is not read as "every run brings new tao files". Nothing was run against homebase or orca; stand-in ssh and rsync pin the arguments. Tests: `tests/test_runners.py::test_r11_m18_*` (8 bad orca addresses, 4 good ones, 9 bad workers, 8 bad keys, the good key forms). | 614 | `hycom._chunks_dir` takes a directory `c` as the chunk directory of a Zarr 3 array only when it holds whole-number names (`[0-9]+`) and nothing else. A Zarr 3 array written with the `.` separator keeps its chunk files in the array directory, not under `c/`, so the rule does not touch it. | 621 | The runners do not inherit the host's environment, and the RUNBOOK says so (step 1, after the `pm()` definition): `docker run` passes only its `-e` list, `bin/pmx.sh` and `./pm.sh` send a fixed list, and the proot runner is `env -i` with a fixed list and sets `CAFE_WORKERS` from `PM_CPUS`; so `CAFE_OFFLINE=1`, `CAFE_WORKERS`, `CAFE_NPROC` and `CAFE_EMULATOR_TABLE` set in the host shell never reach the job. The documented form is `env VAR=value` in front of the command (`pm env CAFE_OFFLINE=1 python -m cafe.pm run ...`, `bin/pmx.sh env CAFE_OFFLINE=1 python ...`), or `-e VAR=value` in the `docker run` line of `pm()`. The forwarding option (a shared allowlist in all runners) was not chosen: it would weaken the `env -i` hermeticity (a forwarded host `CAFE_WORKERS` could exceed the `--cpus` and `--memory` limits the plan assumes; a host `CAFE_OFFLINE` could switch a lab run offline silently). A test (`tests/test_config_docs.py::test_r11_m29_*`) runs the real `bin/proot_run.sh` with a stand-in proot that executes the job's own command and checks both halves: the documented `env` form reaches the job's environment (CAFE_OFFLINE and CAFE_WORKERS), and a host variable does not. | Review round 11, M-29 (B-1), the lead's decision: documents only. Reproduced exactly on orca (proot, `PM_CPUS=2`): `CAFE_OFFLINE=1 CAFE_WORKERS=1 CAFE_EMULATOR_TABLE=/x bin/pmx.sh python <script printing os.environ.get of the four>` printed `[None, '2', None, None]` (the 2 is `PM_CPUS`); `bin/pmx.sh env CAFE_OFFLINE=1 python ...` printed `['1', '2', None, None]`. The docker path was read, not run: its line has a fixed `-e` list and the host environment does not reach the remote. The runner is hermetic on purpose and the variables are honoured once inside the container; DECISIONS 21, 99 and 330 do not say another route is needed. Before the sentence the offline-refusal row of the troubleshooting table was unreachable by following the RUNBOOK literally; the pointers are in step 0, step 1, step 5 (the emulator table) and the three troubleshooting rows. |

| 632 | A calm sea of the ingest checks (598) is a sea with no waves and no wind: a sampled point is calm when `swh` is at or below `CALM_SWH_M` (1e-3 m) and the larger of WRF's 10 m wind there and the wave file's own `wspd` (`pipeline._sample_wind`; NaN when neither is known, which is not calm) is at most `CALM_WIND_MS` (0.2 m/s; a fully developed sea of 0.21 U^2 / g is 1 mm high at 0.22 m/s). Zero waves under a wind are a wave file that is zero or was never written, and it is a share, not all-or-half: the wave file is broken when more than `FLAT_SHARE_MAX` (0.01, an assumption: a sheltered node or a cold-started point is no wipe) of the sampled sea points have `swh` at or below `CALM_SWH_M` under a wind above `CALM_WIND_MS`; then the three `spectrum_*_vs_file` checks fail naming it ("N of M sampled sea points have swh at or below 0.001 m under a wind above 0.2 m/s ...: the wave file is zero or was never written, which is no calm"), whatever the other points say. The u* check fails by the same share for a UMWM `ust` at or below `USTAR_FLOOR_MS` under a wind: `_ustar_agreement` counts such a point as an infinite relative difference (so it is compared and disagrees, not skipped) and returns that count as its fifth value. A real calm (everything zero, wind included) still passes with no value. A UMWM hour that is zero at every point because the run started from rest now fails the checks too: the sample cannot tell it from a wipe. Measured on the real q11 cache (orca, 2 CPUs): the u* agreement is 888 sampled sea points, median 0.0398, 98.76 % within 20 %, and the spectrum errors (906 points) have no flat point (0 of 906), so no printed number of the demo moves; `ingest` was not rerun on that cache with the share rule. | 652 | The reflection coefficient of `comms.two_ray_curved_db` stays -1; the claim that it is valid for grazing angles below about 1 to 2 degrees is withdrawn. The demo's grazing angles (UAS 18 to 108 m, whip 2.2 m) are 1.2 deg at 1 km and 0.2 deg at 5 km for the 18 m UAS and 6.3 and 1.3 deg for the 108 m UAS, and 2 to 30 deg on the sortie hook. The error band of the margin against Fresnel, as the reviewer measured it: the -1 model is optimistic at interference peaks by up to 7.6 dB and conservative at nulls; Fresnel minus -1 on the mission link (0.2 km to the horizon) is -7.4 to +23.6 dB at Hs 0.5 m and -2.8 to +6.9 dB at Hs 1.5 m, on the hook (0.05 to 6 km, Hs 0.5 m) -7.5 to +34.7 dB, with no sample changing sign. | 653 | COARE uses the wave-age and sea-state roughness in full only where UMWM's dominant phase speed is at least `coare.CP_FULL` = 3.5 m/s; between `CP_MIN` = 2.0 m/s and 3.5 m/s the Charnock parameter is blended linearly from the Charnock value to that form (`wmix`), and at 2 m/s or under, negative, tiny or missing (NaN) it is the Charnock form. `coare35` is the only consumer, so this is the one place that picks the roughness form. The two constants and the mid-fade drag are pinned by literals (`tests/test_coare.py::test_r13_m4_*`, row 696). (amends 29; amended by 696) | Review round 12, M-13 (B-7). A UMWM `dcp` of 0 (calm, ice, a fill value) gave NaN u*, z0, L and Cd in derived.zarr and in every profile and turbulence call; the verifier found cp = 0.001 and -1 NaN too, and cp = 0.5 gave u* 4.5 m/s and Cd 0.32. The first pass floored cp at 2.0 with a hard switch; the adversary showed that was a step in Cd of 1.19e-3 to 3.07e-3 (U 8 m/s, sigH 1.5), 1.05e-3 to 1.91e-3 (U 3) and, without sigH, 2.37e-3 to 3.55e-3 (U 20), up to 2.6 times, and that a floor of 3 would only move the step. So the cause was the step, and the fade removes it. The two speeds are the builder's, chosen under the 3.93 m/s that is the smallest of the demo's 5,900,050 finite `dcp` values (measured on the q12 cache), so `wmix` is exactly 1.0 there and the result is bit-for-bit the pure form (a test holds `CP_FULL` at or under 3.93); the receipts use cp 6 to 14. The linear fade guards against the step and is not a calibration of young-sea drag. `sigH` needs no guard (read only where `wmix` is above 0; sigH 0 is finite). No printed number of the demo moves; the derived layer rebuilds once because the code digest changes. | 655 | Member wind error (the bias and the AR(1) noise) is a factor on the base wind vector and is not applied where the base speed is 1e-9 m/s or less: a window of exactly zero wind has no wind spread, and at 1.1e-9 m/s the full bias applies. Documented in the header of `perturb.py`, a comment on `np.where(sp > 1e-9, ...)`, PHYSICS 8 and the CREDIBILITY limitations; not changed. (relates to 598 and 632) | Review round 12, M-15 (B-9), partly confirmed. With a zero base wind and a +1.5 m/s bias all members have speed 0; at 1e-8 m/s the same bias gives 1.5 m/s along the base's arbitrary direction (stub base environment, 4 members: u = 0, 1e-10 and 1e-9 give 0, 1.1e-9 gives 1.5). Wind direction is undefined at zero, so staying zero is one defensible limit and any additive scheme has some jump; a seeded-direction convention would change every small-wind number and the calm-window verdicts, and is not recommended. Exactly zero wind comes from a fixture or a synthetic calm window, which the ingest accepts on purpose (RUNBOOK 3, rows 598 and 632). `current()` is multiplicative, so a zero current staying zero is right. No pin test (the finding offered one, not asked for). |

| 665 | `remote.download(url, path, sha=None, pin=False)` resumes a `.part` only when its record `<name>.part.id` (JSON: total size, ETag and Last-Modified when the server sends them, written from the first probe) equals what the server sends now, and asks for the tail with `If-Range` (the ETag, or Last-Modified when the ETag is weak or absent: `remote._if_range`). No record, an unreadable one, another version, or a reply to the resume that is not 206 or whose identity (`remote._ident`: total from Content-Range, ETag, Last-Modified) differs from the probe's restarts from byte 0; the record is deleted on `os.replace`. `RemoteFile` (the HDF5 block cache of `orca.wrf_grid`, `orca.wrf_hour` and `bathy.fetch_gebco`) compares every ranged reply's identity with the probe's and raises `ConnectionError ... the file changed on the server during this session`. A `.part` of v1 resumed after upstream became v2 gave `v1[:k] + v2[k:]`, which `fetch_tao` hashed into the manifest and `tao.verify` then passed. The first pass relied on `If-Range`; a read-only probe of the real servers on 2026-11-08 (two one-byte ranged GETs each, then one with `If-Range`) showed orca.atmos.washington.edu and dap.ceda.ac.uk send a strong ETag and Last-Modified and honour `If-Range`, while dods.ndbc.noaa.gov (THREDDS, the TAO files) sends Last-Modified only and **ignores `If-Range`** (a wrong value still answers 206, P-R12E1); so a file replaced between the probe and the resume would still have been spliced, and the second pass compares the resume reply's own identity. None of the four sends a checksum header, so "server checksum not checked" is not a defect today. 19's "a stalled zero-byte download raises and keeps its `.part`" stays true; the kept `.part` is reused only while its record matches. Not covered: `cdf1_header` and `cdf1_read` (682). `hycom`, `bathy`, `ocean` and `ledger.py` write `.part` files fresh and never resume. Cost: none in requests. Tests: `tests/test_remote.py::test_r12_m25_*`. |

| 693 | The chance figure printed beside the BRIEF 2.7 coverage gate models the conformal coverage as random. Split-conformal coverage with `n_cal` calibration scores is Beta(k, n_cal + 1 - k), k = `emulator.conformal_rank(n_cal, nominal)` = ceil((n_cal + 1) x nominal) (the one rank rule: `Emulator._conformal_q` uses it too), so the hits on `n_test` held-out points are Beta-Binomial(n_test, k, n_cal + 1 - k), not Binomial(n_test, 0.9). `whatif._chance_miss(n_test, nominal, n_cal=None)` sums that distribution (scipy `betabinom`) over the hit counts outside the unchanged gate set `_coverage_in` (row 540); k above n_cal means an infinite interval, coverage 1, and is explicit (1.0 when 1.0 is outside the band); with no `n_cal` it is the old Binomial and says so. `Emulator.holdout_report` returns `n_cal`, `fit_class` stores `n_cal` and `coverage_chance_model` beside `coverage_chance_fail` in `holdout.json`. The gate, its limits and every verdict are unchanged (BRIEF 2.7): a MISSED coverage result is weaker evidence of miscalibration than rows 263 and 540 said. At 20 test points and 16 calibration scores a calibrated emulator misses the gate with probability 0.527, not 0.2545. The calibration scores are counted as exchangeable rows (`n_cal` is the number of scores, as `_conformal_q` counts them); replicate rows of one point would make the true spread larger still. (amends 263 and 540) | Review round 13, M-1 (D-1, the one major finding; severity raised by the verifier: a wrong printed number stored as `coverage_chance_fail`). A Monte Carlo of 100k split-conformal draws gave 0.526 at 20 (the reviewer's 0.76 and 0.68 at 25 and 30 kept n_cal at 16; the figures here grow n_cal with the table). No demo number moves: q13's `holdout.json` has no trained scope (7 rows of 100); a 4 h or overnight run prints about 0.57 at 28 points where it printed 0.357. Readers of `coverage_chance_fail` (`pipeline.s_emulator` note, `report._chance`, `report.py` 999 with `_opt`) take a number, so older runs still read. Tests: `tests/test_whatif.py::test_round_13_m1_*` (four: an exact Fraction oracle written in the test, a seeded simulation, k above n_cal, the no-`n_cal` label), `tests/test_emulator.py::test_round_13_m1_*`; moved, not weakened (the Binomial figure was the defect): `test_a_trained_emulator_reports_its_holdout_and_acceptance` and `test_a_hold_out_coverage_of_17_of_20_passes_the_gate_and_the_receipt_bracket_agrees`. | 750 | `remote._fits` (a variable's data lies inside the file) skips a record variable whose file has `numrecs` 0 and no longer adds one record to a variable's end (`end = offset + n + (nrec - 1) x recsize` for `nrec >= 1`): the classic format puts no record there, so a file with an unlimited dimension that nothing was written to (a model output still being written, an empty extraction) opens and its fixed variables (lat, lon) can be read. A record variable cut inside its last record is still refused, and `cdf1_read` still refuses a record read of such a file (`IndexError: record 0 outside 0..0`). (amends 707) | Look-ahead L1 after the round 13 merge, M-15. `_fits` had added one full record to the end of every record variable and refused the header ('its data would end at byte 164, past the end of the file (148 bytes)'). Test: `tests/test_remote.py::test_r13_l1_a_valid_file_with_an_unlimited_dimension_nothing_was_written_to_still_opens` (both formats; numrecs 0, 1, 2; a cut file is still refused). |
| 751 | `remote.parse_cdf1` bounds the number of entries a header promises: a list of dimensions, attributes or variables, or the dimension ids of one variable, may hold at most `remote.MAX_ENTRIES` = 65,536 (NetCDF's own limits are 1024 to 8192), else the header is damaged (ValueError). `_Buf.count(most=None)` raises for a count above `most`, and `_Buf.kind` and the rank count of a variable pass it. The per-field cap `MAX_ITEM` (64 MiB) is unchanged; the real restart header and the real files have a few dozen entries. (amends 707) | Look-ahead L1 after the round 13 merge, M-15. Row 707 bounded one field's size and a count's sign but not how many entries a count promises: a count of 2**31 - 1 on a big file with bytes behind it was read to the end of the file into lists (a 4 MB file: 1.0 s and 43 MB, about 11 MB of memory per MB of file; the 2.4 GB UMWM restart would have needed about 25 GB) before the EOFError. It is now refused at once, having fetched only the first 64 kB. Test: `tests/test_remote.py::test_r13_l1_a_hostile_entry_count_is_refused_at_once_not_after_reading_the_file` (dimensions, attributes, variables, the ids of a variable: a ValueError, under 1 MB fetched from a 4 MB file; a header at the cap is accepted). |

| 753 | The stage-3 ledger records `config/assumptions.yaml` by the sha-256 of its values, not of its bytes: `Ledger.input_values(path)` records the file by `ledger.values_sha256` (the parsed mapping, read through `config.read_yaml`, without the fields in `ledger.PROSE_KEYS`, `why` and `replace_with`) with the input key `values_only: true`; `ledger.input_sha(i, f)` is the one place a file input is compared with what was recorded, used by `ledger.done` (resume) and `audit._stage_problems`. A value, a unit, a key, an added field or a damaged file (a file that cannot be read as a mapping hashes to the empty string, which no recorded hash equals) still does. `pipeline_windows.run` records the file with `input_values`; every other input (platform files, `radios.yaml`, `modems.yaml`, `mission.yaml`, `plan.json`, the JSON outputs, `stage1/res.pkl`) is hashed by its bytes as before. A ledger written before this holds the byte hash and cannot be told from one that records a real change: q11, q12, q12la, q13 and q13la read 'changed since the stage ran' after any edit of the file, a wording edit as well as a value one, and only a rerun on the current tree cures it. Turning that into a note would hide a real value edit (`stage3.fea_min_s` 8.0 to 68.2, row 741, is one) from the audit, which is the check's job. The 'q13 audit: 0 problems' line of `review/round_13/q13_vs_q12la.md` was true at `cdad21a` and does not reproduce on this tree. `PROSE_KEYS` is the list of fields no reader uses: a new reader of `why` or `replace_with` (the credibility page reads them at render time, not as a stage) would have to leave the list, or its text would stop being checked. | 754 | The number of emulator variants a run ran follows the machine's speed (the kill before a variant at the stage's share, row 288 stays): on the demo two runs of the same code and inputs can differ in `rows_added`, the points per emulator scope, the flip candidates and tried moves, and `run_note`. | 755 | `current_correlation_length` is checked whatever its `dist`: `sampling.check_entry` requires it to be a fixed setting with a finite mean strictly between `ELL_MIN` 1e-200 and `ELL_MAX` 1e200 m (the first branch of the check; every other fixed entry keeps the finite and above 0 test when it has a mean). `PerturbedEnv` reads it as `priors[...]['mean']` and the random-field generator divides by it, so any other dist gave a KeyError at the first environment, and a mean below about 1e-308 made 1/length infinite and every current field NaN.
